**2026-07-05 — Delayed WhatsApp Admin Notifications & Recheck**
- **Decision**: Added a customizable delay (`delay_minutes`) under `whatsapp_notifications` configuration, defaulting to 5 minutes.
- **Decision**: Added a numeric delay input field to the client's Settings panel (`NotificationConfigPanel.tsx`) allowing them to customize the duration (in minutes) or disable it (0 minutes).
- **Decision**: Added validation checks after the configured delay to query `lead_stage_events` and `leads` to verify that the lead stayed in the target segment continuously. If not, the notification is skipped.
- **Rationale**: Gives clients granular control over alert responsiveness while protecting admins from notification fatigue during rapid lead state transitions.

**2026-07-05 — Fixed: code was overriding tenant AI Tune language-mirroring rules with a wrong hardcoded tag**
- **Symptom, reported by user with two real conversation screenshots**: a tenant with a detailed custom AI Tune prompt (explicit "Dynamic Language Mirroring Rules" + few-shot examples for Tanglish/Pure Tamil/Pure English) still got English or Tamil-script-mixed replies to Tanglish messages ("unge service paththi sollunge", "hi en peru prem en life paththi konjam solle mudiyuma").
- **Root cause, found by testing the actual message-construction pipeline, not guessing**: `generate_reply()` was rewriting the customer's message before it ever reached the model — `f"[Respond in {_detect_lang(message)}] {message}"` — using `_detect_lang()`'s hardcoded `_TANGLISH_MARKERS` keyword list. Both real messages used words not in that list ("paththi", "konjam", "solle", "mudiyuma", "unge", "sollunge" vs. the list's "sollunga"/"unga" etc.), so detection said "en", and the model was handed an explicit `[Respond in English]` instruction sitting directly next to the tenant's own more general (and correct) language rules. The model reasonably treated the more specific, more local instruction as authoritative.
- **Verified the fix before writing code**: ran the tenant's actual full AI Tune prompt + the raw customer message through Sarvam directly, with no injected tag at all. 3/3 runs produced correct, natural Tanglish, matching the tenant's own few-shot example style — proving the model needs zero help from the code's heuristic when the tenant (or the generic fallback prompt) already gives language instructions.
- **Fix**: removed the tag-injection entirely from `generate_reply()` — the raw message now reaches the model unmodified. `_detect_lang()` is kept (still legitimately used to pick which language's canned fallback text to show if the LLM call fails outright — a harmless use, just choosing between a few pre-written strings). The now-fully-unused `_LANG_NAMES` dict was removed along with the test that only asserted its own shape.
- **Also fixed in the same commit, separate concern**: `sarvam_chat_completion()` now defaults `frequency_penalty=0.5`. Testing surfaced Sarvam-30B occasionally (~1/10 replies) degenerating into verbatim sentence-fragment repetition; 0/10 with this parameter set, same reply quality otherwise.
- **Tests**: static source-inspection tests added (matching this codebase's existing convention in `test_ai_reply_quota_enforcement.py`/`test_logic_contracts_static.py`) — `generate_reply`'s source must not contain `"[Respond in"`, module source must not contain `"_LANG_NAMES"`. Full backend suite: 338/338 passing. Pushed to `origin/main` (`9d72ac9`).
 
---
 
**2026-07-05 — Table-Backed Persistent Queue for WhatsApp Delay Notifications**
- **Decision**: Migrated the WhatsApp admin delay notification system from volatile, in-memory `asyncio.sleep()` tasks to a persistent, database-backed queue table `pending_whatsapp_alerts` (Migration 131).
- **Decision**: Registered a periodic scheduler job `pending-whatsapp-alerts` running every 1 minute under the `AsyncIOScheduler` instance in `app/main.py` to process due notifications.
- **Decision**: Replaced the deprecated `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-capable">` tag with `<meta name="mobile-web-app-capable" content="yes">` and removed the orphaned Vercel Speed Insights script dependency from `layout.tsx` to resolve console 404 errors.
- **Rationale**: In-memory background tasks are lost during Render restarts and cold-start sleep cycles. A table-backed queue ensures 100% crash-proof delivery. Speed Insights was throwing 404 errors since the production app is hosted on Render instead of Vercel.

**2026-07-06 — WhatsApp voice replies (Sarvam TTS) + language-switch instruction rework, migration collision resolved**
- **Decision**: Shipped `send_whatsapp_voice_reply()` in `ai_reply.py` — replies go out as a Sarvam TTS audio message instead of text when the new per-tenant `ai_voice_reply_enabled` setting is on, with `_tts_language_code()` mapping `_detect_lang()`'s output to a Sarvam TTS language code. Falls back to the normal text `send_whatsapp()` if the voice send fails or the setting is off.
- **Migration collision found and resolved**: the user's local `131_add_ai_voice_reply_setting.sql` and a teammate's already-`origin/main`-merged `131_pending_whatsapp_alerts.sql` both used number 131. Renumbered the local one to `132_add_ai_voice_reply_setting.sql` before applying. Also discovered `pending_whatsapp_alerts` (table + RLS + policy + both indexes) was already live on Supabase despite not appearing in `list_migrations`' tracked history — the teammate's migration content had already been applied out-of-band. Re-running it verbatim would have failed on the non-idempotent `CREATE POLICY`. Applied only the genuinely new `132_add_ai_voice_reply_setting.sql`.
- **Fix, language-switch instruction**: reworded the generic LANGUAGE RULE's trailing clause from a prohibition ("Never switch styles unless the user explicitly asks you to") to a positive, trigger-based instruction ("If the customer's latest message explicitly requests a different language ... you MUST fully switch"). Verified live: 3/3 correct switches to English on an explicit "in English please" request, vs. 1/3 with the old prohibition wording, with no false-positive switching (3/3 correctly stayed Tanglish) when no switch was requested.
- **New unresolved finding (not fixed, needs a different approach)**: when a customer naturally switches from Tanglish to pure Tamil script mid-conversation *without* an explicit request, the model does NOT reliably mirror the script change — 0/3 in live testing, even after generalizing the rule to also cover implicit script mismatches (that generalized wording also slightly regressed the explicit-request case, 2/3 vs 3/3). This looks like Sarvam-30B's own-prior-reply style anchoring outweighing system-prompt instructions for silent (non-verbal) switches, distinct from the explicit-request case which the model follows well. Proposed next options (not yet approved/built): (a) accept as a known limitation, (b) shrink conversation history sent to the model to weaken the anchor, (c) add a deterministic post-reply script-mismatch check (Unicode block comparison, not a guess) that triggers one regeneration attempt, (d) flag for human handover on mismatch instead of auto-correcting.
- **Also found, config not code**: a tenant's own phone number was configured as the `recipient_phones` target for the `whatsapp_notifications` admin segment-change alert (see subsystem-notes.md → WhatsApp templates & admin notifications), while also being a live test lead in the same tenant — admin alerts about *other* leads' segment changes were landing in that same person's personal WhatsApp thread, using a customer-facing template (`aira_connect`), making it look like a broken duplicate-reply bug. Root-caused via direct DB queries (recipient config, lead records, `pending_whatsapp_alerts` timestamps matching exactly to other leads' real segment_changed events) — not a code bug. User to reconfigure `recipient_phones`/template themselves.
- **Tests**: 344/344 backend passing, frontend typecheck clean. Committed and merged with a concurrent teammate push (`whatsapp_notify.py`/`pending_whatsapp_alerts` admin-alert feature) with no conflicts. Pushed to `origin/main`.


**2026-07-06 - Telecalling queue prioritizes message-led leads before CSV upload leads**
- **Decision**: The caller To Call queue is now one pipeline with two visible source sections: "Inbound & outbound message leads" first and "Telecalling upload leads" second. Message-led leads include native channel leads (`whatsapp`, `instagram`, `facebook`, `telegram`) and uploaded leads that later receive inbound activity.
- **Implementation**: `frontend/app/dashboard/telecalling/lib/queue-priority.ts` sorts the visible queue by source group, segment, score, and latest activity. `backend/app/routes/calls.py::_call_queue_sort_key` mirrors the same priority for `GET /api/v1/calls/next-lead`, covering both unassigned pool claims and assigned fallback queue selection.
- **Rationale**: Uploaded CSV contacts give telecallers a base worklist, but active inbound/outbound-message conversations are warmer and more time-sensitive; they must jump above uploaded contacts without creating a separate telecalling workflow.

**2026-07-07 — Robust Multilingual Chat Escalation Trigger Improvements**
- **Decision**: Replaced rigid keyword matching with robust, Unicode-aware regular expression classifiers for user human requests (`_HUMAN_REQUEST_RE`) and AI escalation-indicative replies (`_AI_ESCALATION_RE`) in [ai_reply.py](file:///c:/Users/vskee/Desktop/Aira.AI/Aira-Ai/backend/app/services/ai_reply.py).
- **Implementation**:
  - `_HUMAN_REQUEST_RE` was expanded to match flexible English structures (e.g. `"speak with your owner"`, `"i need to talk with your team"`, `"contact me"`, `"call me"`), Romanized regional Indian languages/Tanglish/Hinglish/Tenglish (e.g. `"owner-kitta pesanum"`, `"mujhe agent se baat karni hai"`, `"call pannunga"`, `"matladali"`), and native scripts (Tamil, Hindi, Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam). It omits strict word boundaries (`\b`) on native Indic characters to resolve Unicode matching issues.
  - `_AI_ESCALATION_RE` matches AI follow-up / human-connect responses with flexible verb tenses and helper verb variations (e.g. `"I need to confirm with our team regarding the partial payment."`, `"I've informed my team..."`, and `"My team will assist you..."`).
  - Updated Trigger F check inside `generate_reply` to evaluate `_AI_ESCALATION_RE.search(reply_text)` instead of the old static string list comparison.
- **Verification**: Created a new test suite `tests/test_escalation_triggers.py` validating English, Romanized Indian languages, native Indic scripts, and AI follow-up variations. All 4 new tests and the complete suite of 354 backend tests passed successfully (`pytest` inside venv).

**2026-07-13 — AI reply language mirroring: two failed preemptive attempts, one working post-reply fix, and a newly found gap**
- **Context**: continuation of the 2026-07-06 silent-script-switch finding above. User's working theory was "tell the model preemptively, don't do a post-reply check" — tested both, with real live API calls, not reasoning.
- **Attempt 1 (failed, live 0/6)**: added `_dominant_script()` (deterministic Unicode-block script count, no keyword guessing) and `_latest_message_script_note()`, injecting an explicit per-turn "CUSTOMER'S LATEST MESSAGE SCRIPT: ..." tag into the system prompt, tested at two placements (top of prompt; immediately adjacent to the final user turn). Silent switches still failed 0/6 across both. Explicit-request and no-switch cases stayed correct, unaffected. Code kept (harmless), but superseded.
- **Attempt 2 (working fix, live 18/18)**: `_reply_script_mismatch()` compares the generated reply's script against the customer's latest message *after* generation, before send; `generate_reply()` fires ONE regeneration call on mismatch via `_regen_target_instruction()`. Two details were required for this to actually work: (1) the regen call carries **zero conversation history** — an isolated "rewrite this text" framing, not "redo your last turn" (the history-inclusive version tested first only fixed 1/6, since re-showing the wrong reply + full history re-triggers the same anchor); (2) the target language must be **named explicitly** ("English or Tanglish"), not "Latin script" — a vague script-only instruction let one regen drift into literal Latin (the dead language), since any Latin-alphabet text technically satisfies "not Tamil script". `_is_explicit_language_switch_request()` exempts explicit requests from this check entirely, so the already-working case is untouched.
- **New gap found while re-verifying "unaffected" cases**: re-testing the explicit-request case ("tamil-la sollunga") live, today, got **0/3** — the model did not switch to Tamil script despite the explicit request, directly contradicting the 2026-07-06 finding of 3/3 on the same nominal scenario. The exemption in `_reply_script_mismatch()` means this case currently gets zero protection from either fix. Root cause of the false "PASS" in my own first report: the verification script's success criterion compared the reply's script to the customer's *own message's* script (Latin, since "tamil-la sollunga" is itself written in roman letters) rather than to the *requested* language — a scoring bug that hid a real behavior gap. Not yet fixed — proposed approach (extract the requested language from the regex match, apply the same mismatch-check-and-regen pattern using that as the target) needs the request-detection regex tightened first, since a naive version would false-positive on sentences that merely mention a language (e.g. "I live in Tamil Nadu") and — unlike today's exemption-only behavior — would then actively force a wrong language switch rather than just skipping a safety net. See active-backlog.md.
- **Verification**: unit tests 27/27 (`test_ai_reply_lang_detection.py`), full backend suite 405/406 (the 1 failure is the pre-existing, unrelated `test_permission_gated_routers` regression noted above). Live verification used the actual production functions end to end against real Sarvam-30B, not mocks.
- **Rule reinforced**: never trust a prompt-engineering or regen-design claim on reasoning alone, including "just add a computed tag" — run it live 3+ times per scenario, read the raw output text (not just an automated pass/fail bucket), and re-verify previously-confirmed cases too, since model behavior was found to be inconsistent across otherwise-identical scenarios six days apart.

**2026-07-13 — Catalog item disambiguation (semantic retrieval + variant grouping)**
- **Problem**: the AI catalog-recommendation feature (`_build_catalog_context` in `ai_reply.py`) had no way to tell "one clear match" from "the customer's message matches several variants of the same item" (e.g. a real-estate tenant's "2BHK Apartment" listed in 3 cities, or a bakery's cake flavors) — it dumped the whole catalog into the prompt every turn and hoped the model wouldn't over-recommend. Also, `max_images_per_reply` was read from an unvalidated settings payload and never actually enforced as a cap in code.
- **Decision**: reused the existing Knowledge Base RAG stack (Jina embeddings, pgvector, `match_knowledge_chunks`-style RPC pattern) rather than a new provider. Migration `140_catalog_disambiguation.sql` added `catalog_variant_groups` + `attributes`/`variant_group_id`/`embedding` columns on `catalog_items`, plus `match_catalog_items`/`update_catalog_item_embedding` RPCs — applied live to Supabase (`ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`), verified via direct SQL query.
- **Implementation**: `backend/app/services/catalog_retrieval.py` (new) holds the pure gate logic (`contention_set`, `classify_gate` → `confident`/`same_group`/`broad_browse`, `differing_attribute_directive`, `broad_browse_directive`) plus retrieval/embedding functions (`match_catalog_items`, `embed_and_store_catalog_item`, `reindex_catalog_items`). `ai_reply.py`'s `_build_catalog_context` is now async, retrieval-based, fails open to the old full-catalog-dump behavior on any provider/RPC error. The WhatsApp send loop now hard-slices `catalog_images_to_send[:catalog_max_images]`. `routes/catalog.py` accepts `attributes`/`variant_group_id` on create/update (embeds synchronously, fail-open), adds `POST /catalog/reindex`, and — added in a post-review fix round — `POST`/`GET /catalog/variant-groups` (without this, the FK on `variant_group_id` made grouping unreachable via API entirely). Frontend: `dashboard/catalog/page.tsx` got a Variant Groups panel (create/list, item counts) and an Attributes key/value editor + variant-group picker inside the item add/edit modal (renamed `AddItemModal` → `AddEditItemModal`, edit support is new).
- **Backward-compat fix mid-flight**: `classify_gate` originally gated legacy/ungrouped tenants (all `variant_group_id: None`) into `broad_browse` whenever 2+ ordinary items matched closely — a real regression from "recommend one, send its photo." Fixed: an all-`None` contention set now classifies as `confident`, and — after a second finding — `confident` now passes the FULL contention set to the prompt (not just the top-ranked item), so a legacy tenant's multi-item browse query still sees everything relevant, matching (arguably improving on) pre-feature behavior. `broad_browse`/`same_group` only ever trigger once real grouping data exists among the candidates.
- **Known, accepted risk (not fixed)**: partial embedding coverage — see active-backlog.md. Two other minor gaps (mixed grouped/ungrouped contention losing the differentiator; a stale `reindex_catalog_items` docstring) also left open, also in active-backlog.md.
- **Process note**: built via `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` — 5 plan tasks + a final whole-branch review (Opus) that caught two Important cross-task gaps a narrow per-task lens missed (the variant-group FK-reachability gap above, and the confident-branch top-1-only narrowing). Both were fixed and re-verified in a second review pass before shipping.
- **Concurrent collaboration incident (informational, not a bug)**: a teammate (git author `keerthi-sarav`) was actively pushing directly to `origin/main` throughout this session. Three separate `git pull --rebase origin main` events absorbed their commits (a `rbac.py` crash fix, an `.agents/` status update, and a fix to `test_security_hardening_static.py` that resolved the previously-tracked `test_permission_gated_routers` failure) cleanly into this branch's history, replaying all of this feature's commits on top each time — content verified intact after each rebase, only hashes changed. The implementation plan doc (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-13-catalog-item-disambiguation.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history)) was never committed during the multi-hour build and was found missing from disk after one such incident (a concurrent working-tree operation, not git, must have removed it) — recreated verbatim and committed. **Lesson: commit plan/scratch docs immediately after writing them when working directly on a shared `main` with concurrent collaborators, don't leave them as untracked working-tree files.**
- **Verification**: 436/436 backend tests passing pre-frontend-work; frontend `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean (only pre-existing unrelated warnings in other files). Migration verified live via direct SQL query (columns, RPCs, and the new table all confirmed present).

**2026-07-13 - Settings menu RBAC placement and dashboard overview retry**
- **Decision**: Settings remains out of the assigned-user sidebar. The avatar/profile menu is the shared Settings entry point: owners and roles with either `settings.view` or `settings.manage` see the item there.
- **Decision**: A `500` from the read-only `/api/v1/analytics/overview` aggregate is retried through the existing GET retry policy before the dashboard renders its error card. Other application `500` responses are not globally retried.
- **Live investigation**: Render `/health` returned `200`; Supabase project `Aira AI` was healthy; `tenant_roles`, RBAC membership columns, and the analytics query columns existed with RLS enabled; no current API/Postgres failure was present. The observed dashboard error was therefore transient rather than a persistent schema/access failure.
- **Verification**: `frontend npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. The local frontend changes still require deployment before Bloom Matrix reflects them.

**2026-07-13 - Dashboard read-only RBAC enforcement: workflows, subscriptions, team, and conversations**
- **Decision**: Read access remains navigable and inspectable; write actions are visibly disabled and backend-protected. This applies to Segments/Re-engagement message creation, Outbound tags and broadcasts, Templates submission, Subscription requests, Team shift/holiday controls, and Conversations.
- **Permission model**: `require_permission()` now treats `*.manage` as satisfying the corresponding `*.view`. `conversations.reply` additionally implies `conversations.view`, matching the existing sidebar behavior so a reply-capable role can load the inbox. Mutations require their dedicated manage/reply permission; view-only API access cannot mutate by direct request.
- **Conversations implementation**: Archive, escalation Resolve, escalation Pick up/Reply, text reply, and media reply require `conversations.reply`. View-only users see Archive/Resolve and reply controls disabled with a read-only tooltip; the conversation and escalation read endpoints require `conversations.view`.
- **Re-engagement failure fix**: Re-engagement sequence reads use `leads.view`, while create/delete/trigger mutations use `leads.manage`, preventing the prior failed-sequence response for valid read roles and preserving outbound-leads-only write scope.
- **Schema**: No migration was created or applied. The live Supabase schema already contains the tables/columns used by these changes, including `tenant_roles`, `reengagement_steps`, `broadcast_tags`, caller shifts, and attendance overrides.
- **Verification**: frontend `npm run typecheck`, Python `py_compile` for affected backend routes, and `git diff --check` passed. A local Next production build did not return a final result because overlapping Node/Next workers remained active; it emitted only existing Sentry configuration warnings and must be rerun in a clean process state before deployment.

- **Decision**: The role editor uses a desktop viewport-based height with a fixed header/footer and an independently scrollable module/resource matrix. Compact spacing keeps at least five resources visible before scrolling.

**2026-07-18 — Sarvam STT/doc-digitization + analyze_call migrated to Gemini 3.1 Flash-Lite; ai_reply_model fallback removed; daily digest bug fixed**
- **Context**: continuation of the 2026-07-05 Groq→Sarvam migration (active-backlog.md), which had explicitly kept `analyze_call`/scoring/digest/coaching on Groq. Revisited because Llama 3.3 70B's officially supported languages (English, German, French, Italian, Portuguese, Hindi, Spanish, Thai) don't include Tamil, while the call/message volume here is majority Tamil/Tanglish — a real, previously-unverified quality gap on the highest-stakes reasoning step in the pipeline (scoring criteria like `objection_handling`/`professionalism` need language nuance, not just gist comprehension).
- **Decision**: STT (WhatsApp voice notes + call recordings), doc digitization (scanned PDFs + images in the knowledge base), and `analyze_call` (summary + scoring) all moved from Sarvam/Groq to **Gemini `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`**, all three on the same model (operator chose cost over `2.5 Flash`'s reasoning headroom and `3.5 Flash`'s stronger-but-4-6x-costlier tier — `2.5 Flash` was independently ruled out anyway, see subsystem-notes.md deprecation note). `call_digest.py`/`call_coach.py` were deliberately left on Groq — out of scope, not touched.
- **Pre-migration verification, live**: before writing any integration code, ran a live probe script against the real `/v1beta/interactions` endpoint (not doc assumptions — this endpoint has repeatedly deviated from standard Gemini API docs in this codebase's history) to find: (1) the audio/image/document content-part request shape, (2) whether formal JSON-schema mode works for `analyze_call`'s 20-field payload. Both documented in subsystem-notes.md — the flat `{"type": "audio"|"image"|"document", "data": <base64>, "mime_type": ...}` shape works and was confirmed with real OCR/transcription output; the formal `response_format` schema mode is broken (accepted but never completes, emits unbounded whitespace) and was abandoned in favor of prompt-only JSON + one retry.
- **Implementation**: `gemini_client.py` gained `gemini_speech_to_text()`, `gemini_extract_document_text()` (deliberately **synchronous**, unlike every other function in this file — its only caller, `knowledge_service.extract_text_from_file`, is itself sync and dispatched via `asyncio.to_thread`), and `gemini_chat_completion_json()` (retry-once on parse failure). Five call sites swapped: `knowledge_service.py` (×2), `webhook.py`'s `_transcribe_whatsapp_audio`, `call_summarizer.py`'s `transcribe_recording()` and `analyze_call()`. `sarvam_document_intelligence.py` deleted entirely (the whole create→upload→start→poll→download→unzip job lifecycle no longer needed — Gemini takes inline bytes in one request). `sarvam_speech_to_text()` + `SARVAM_STT_URL` removed from `sarvam_client.py`; Sarvam chat (`sarvam-30b`/`105b` reply models) and Sarvam TTS (already-unused dead code, not cleaned up, see active-backlog.md) untouched.
- **Separate but same-session decision — `ai_reply_model` silent fallback removed**: `sarvam-30b` was believed deprecated at the time. **Correction (2026-07-18): that claim was wrong — sarvam-30b is live and remains one of four equal reply providers (Sarvam/Groq/Gemini/OpenAI), selected per tenant by the operator like any other; commit 3a7807f's message carries the stale rationale.** The decision itself stands independently: rather than pick a new hardcoded default, the operator chose **no silent fallback at all** — `_resolve_reply_model()` now calls `require_tenant_setting("ai_reply_model", tenant_id)` and raises if unconfigured, same contract as every provider API key. Verified safe: `generate_reply()` is already called inside a try/except in `webhook.py` that logs and skips on any exception, the same path a missing API key already took — no new failure mode introduced. `operator.py`'s config-display endpoint stopped reporting a fake `"sarvam-30b"` for unconfigured tenants (now returns the raw `null`); `config.tsx`'s picker stopped labeling that entry "Default. Best fit..." (still selectable, just not recommended). **No existing tenant was affected** — confirmed by the operator before shipping.
- **Bug found and fixed in the same area, unrelated to the migration**: `call_digest.py`'s `generate_daily_digest()` referenced an undefined `date_str` on two lines (one inside the try block, one in the unconditional `caller_digests` upsert) — a `NameError` on every single run, since the function was written, for every caller, every day. Caught by the outer per-caller `try/except` in `generate_digests_for_tenant()` and only logged, so it never surfaced as a visible failure. Root cause: the daily coaching digest has never once successfully written a row to `caller_digests` — every operator/caller who ever opened the "AI Coaching" card only ever saw the frontend's empty-state fallback (a manual "Get Tip" button hitting the separate, weaker on-demand endpoint), believing that was the whole feature. Fix: one line, `date_str = for_date.isoformat()`. Live-verified with a throwaway script exercising the exact crash line (not just re-running the existing unit tests, which never touched `generate_daily_digest` itself) — confirmed no `NameError` and correct `digest_date` value. No frontend or architecture change needed; `CoachingDigest.tsx` already had the right fallback-chain design (digest primary, on-demand tip only when no digest exists yet), it just never got real data to show.
- **Test suite fallout, all fixed same session**: rewrote tests in `test_knowledge_service.py`, `test_call_summarizer_eval_v2.py`, `test_sarvam_chat_completion.py`; added new coverage in `test_gemini_client.py` for all three new functions; deleted `test_sarvam_document_intelligence.py` (tested the deleted module). Also caught two regressions from the `ai_reply_model` fallback change that had never been checked against the suite until this same close-out: `test_ai_reply_llm_wiring.py` had most of its tests silently broken by a shared `require_tenant_setting` mock now colliding across two different keys (`ai_reply_model` vs `{provider}_api_key`) — rewrote with a key-aware `side_effect` helper; `test_operator_client_config.py` still asserted the old "defaults to sarvam-30b" display behavior — updated to assert `null`.
- **Known limitation carried forward, now moot**: the 2026-07-05 backlog entry's "Sarvam Vision's `language` hardcoded to `en-IN`" limitation no longer applies — `sarvam_document_intelligence.py` (the file that limitation lived in) is deleted. Gemini's OCR has no equivalent per-language config surface; not treated as a gap since nothing in this migration regressed multilingual OCR accuracy relative to before (untested either way, no complaints to chase).
- **Verification**: full backend suite 488/488 passing (was 487/488 mid-session on a pre-existing unrelated PYTHONPATH pytest-collection gap in two test files, worked around with `PYTHONPATH=.` for verification, not fixed — see active-backlog.md). Frontend `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Full-repo grep swept for dangling references to every removed Sarvam function/module — none found.

**2026-07-23 — Per-creative Meta Ad attribution + Ad Performance analytics tab (migration 147)**
- **Context**: `ad_campaigns` already tracked campaign-level Meta Ad attribution via CTWA `referral` (see `webhook.py:437-459`), but a tenant running several creatives (videos/posters) under one campaign had no way to see which specific creative drove clicks/messages. Original brainstorm considered a self-hosted redirect-tracking domain to capture individual "clicked but never messaged" events; rejected because it requires switching ads from native Click-to-WhatsApp destination to Website/Traffic, which drops Meta's automatic `referral` object and changes ad-delivery optimization away from message-completion.
- **Decision**: Click counts for "clicked but no message" come from **Meta's Ads Insights API** (`level=ad`, field `inline_link_clicks` — NOT the broader `clicks` field, which includes non-CTA engagement) via a new scheduled job `meta_ads_insights_sync.py` (APScheduler, every 6h, job id `ad-insights-sync`). `ad_creatives` rows are **auto-imported** from the Insights response (keyed on `meta_ad_id`) — no manual creative registration; a tenant-edited `creative_label` is preserved (`label_edited` flag) and never overwritten by future syncs. Per-message attribution extends the existing CTWA referral block in `webhook.py` to also stamp `leads.attributed_ad_creative_id` from `referral.source_id`, guarded to only set when currently NULL (same no-clobber pattern as the existing Google Ads attribution).
- **Credentials**: per-tenant `meta_ads_access_token` / `meta_ads_account_id`, stored via the existing `app_settings` paste-in pattern (same as `meta_access_token`/`instagram_access_token`) — deliberately NOT an OAuth "Connect" flow, since the correct token type (a Business Manager System User token, no expiry) can only be generated manually in Meta Business Manager anyway; a real OAuth consent flow would yield a worse, expiring token. **Gotcha**: the WhatsApp channel's "Validate & Activate" button (`/api/v1/settings/activate`) only tests/saves the WhatsApp-specific keys (`meta_access_token`, `meta_phone_number_id`, `meta_waba_id`, `meta_app_secret`) — it does NOT touch or validate the Ads fields. Those only persist via the generic "Save Changes" → `PATCH /api/v1/settings/`.
- **Scope descoped for this build**: Sales/Revenue/ROAS columns show `—` — `leads` has no conversion/revenue field yet (only segment A/B/C/D scoring). Qualified = segment A/B, Hot = segment A, both real. Meta Conversions API (sending qualified/hot/converted events back to Meta) explicitly deferred to a future phase.
- **Diagnostic addition (same day)**: the 6h scheduler interval plus fully-swallowed per-tenant exceptions (by design, so one tenant's bad token never crashes the shared job) made first-deploy debugging painful — a token was pasted, migration applied, but the first sync ran before credentials existed and the wait for the next natural attempt was opaque. Added `POST /inbound-leads/ad-sync-now` (backed by `sync_tenant_ad_insights_verbose()`, a non-swallowing sibling of the background `sync_tenant_ad_insights()`) plus a "Sync Now" button in the Ad Performance tab, returning `{ok, error, rows_fetched, written}` immediately instead of waiting on the schedule.
- **Placement**: new "Ad Performance" tab inside `frontend/app/dashboard/inbound-leads/` (not the top-level `dashboard/analytics/` page) — inbound-leads already models ad-attributed vs. organic leads via `ad_campaign_id`, the closer conceptual home. Campaign → ad set → creative cascading filters, CSV export.
- **Verification**: live-tested against the real "Astro Tamil" Meta ad account (`act_1910086849857231`) — confirmed `ads_read` scope, per-ad `inline_link_clicks`/`spend`/`adset_id`/`campaign_id` all returned correctly. 589/589 backend tests passing, frontend typecheck/lint/build clean. Merged to `main` directly (additive schema + full test coverage, no PR needed per user's explicit call) as `aeec7e7`. Migration 147 applied to live Supabase and confirmed (`ad_creatives`/`ad_insights_daily` exist) — but as of this entry, `ad_insights_daily` still had 0 rows for the live tenant despite valid credentials; root cause not yet found (Render log search was inconclusive — see active-backlog.md). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-ad-creative-attribution-analytics-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-23-ad-creative-attribution-analytics.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history).

**2026-07-24 — In-dashboard Meta Ads Manager: full-account reporting + Analytics + ad creation/management (migrations 148 + 149)**
- **Context**: builds directly on the 2026-07-23 per-creative attribution work (migration 147). Goal: let clients — who are typically first-time advertisers (the "cafe with 100 WhatsApp contacts" persona) — run and manage Meta Click-to-WhatsApp ads *inside* Aira without ever opening Meta Ads Manager (which is too intimidating for a Meta newcomer). New top-level sidebar page `/dashboard/meta-ads`, distinct from the Inbound Leads → Ad Performance tab (which stays scoped to CTWA-lead attribution and was NOT touched).
- **Decision — split into two plans by blocker, not by feature area**: Plan 1 (Reporting) uses `ads_read` only → no external dependency, shipped immediately. Plan 2 (Create + Management) needs `ads_management` (write) → gated on Meta App Review. They share the page shell + data model, so Plan 1 landed first regardless of the approval timeline.
- **Plan 1 (migration 148)**: widened `ad_insights_daily` (impressions/reach/`actions` jsonb) and `ad_campaigns` (objective/effective_status/daily_budget/lifetime_budget/bid_strategy). Widened `meta_ads_insights_sync.py` to capture the new metric fields **and** added a campaign-level fetch (`GET /act_<id>/campaigns`) storing status/objective/budget on `ad_campaigns`. New `meta_ads_reporting.py` (campaign/adset/ad rollup joining insights × creatives × leads) and `meta_ads_analytics.py` (funnel, cost-per-hot leaderboard, spend-vs-qualified trend, IST day×hour heatmap, spend-efficiency quadrant, spend-distribution donut). New `meta_ads` router (`/performance`, `/analytics`, `/filters`). Page has **Ad Performance** + **Analytics** tabs.
- **Plan 2 (migration 149)**: new `ad_sets` table + create-provenance columns (`ad_campaigns.special_ad_category/created_via/page_id`, `ad_creatives.created_by_aira/prefilled_greeting/media_asset_ref/cta_type`). `meta_ads_payloads.py` (pure, unit-tested CTWA payload builders), `meta_ads_manager.py` (write client + create-orchestrator campaign→adset→creative→ad + persistence + status/budget writes). Write endpoints on the router (`/campaigns`, `/media`, `/pages`, `/whatsapp-numbers`, `/{id}/status`, `/{id}/budget`). New **Create** tab (single-scroll CTWA wizard + live preview). Status-toggle + inline budget-edit added to the Ad Performance table (campaign-level rows only).
- **UI/IA**: the 3 tabs (Create / Ad Performance / Analytics) live in the shared `AppHeader`, driven by the `?tab=` query param — same pattern as Outbound Leads (no `?tab` → Ad Performance default; `?tab=create`/`?tab=analytics`). `MetaAdsClient` reads `searchParams` and swaps the tab body.
- **Scope descoped**: objectives beyond Click-to-WhatsApp (Website Traffic/App/Leads/Sales/Awareness), custom/lookalike audiences, manual placements/interest targeting (Meta itself deprecated Detailed Targeting Jan 2026 → Advantage+ is the default and the omission-of-fields approach), Warm as a distinct funnel stage (Hot only), ROAS/revenue (still no `leads.converted_at`/deal-value — see active-backlog.md).
- **Verification**: backend 611/611 passing (payload builders + rollup + orchestration/persistence all unit-tested with FakeDB + monkeypatched httpx). Frontend lint + typecheck + build clean. Migrations 148 & 149 applied to live Supabase (project ref `ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`) and column/table existence confirmed. Merged to `main` (`939b8f5`) and pushed to origin with explicit user go-ahead; Plan 1 had already reached origin/main via PR #21 from the `ChillandBuild` fork, so the push carried only the header change + Plan 2. Specs/plans: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-24-meta-ads-manager-design.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-24-meta-ads-foundation-reporting.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-24-meta-ads-create-management.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history).
- **REMAINING BLOCKER**: live ad creation/management (`ads_management`) is SUBMITTED but PENDING Advanced Access — see active-backlog.md and subsystem-notes.md.

**2026-07-25 — Fixed WhatsApp delivery-failure misclassification (account lock wrongly blacklisted 56 leads) + 4 related dashboard/inbox bugs**
- **Context**: user reported Dashboard/Segments/Conversations showing three different "Disqualified" counts (8/4/3) for tenant Astro Tamil. Traced live via Supabase queries (not from docs/notes) rather than assumed — user explicitly pushed back on an early guess and demanded empirical verification.
- **Root cause**: `webhook.py`'s delivery-failure handler flagged `leads.whatsapp_undeliverable=true` for **any** non-transient Meta error code. Only `131026` ("wrong number") genuinely means the recipient's number is bad. When the tenant's WhatsApp Business Account got Meta-restricted (error `131031` "Business Account locked" — an account-wide failure, not per-recipient), every AI auto-reply that failed during the restriction wrongly blacklisted that lead's own phone number. Verified via live SQL: **56 leads tenant-wide**, 100% of them flagged by `131031`, zero by a genuine `131026` failure.
- **Fix**: flipped the logic from a deny-list ("blacklist unless known-transient") to an allow-list — `PERMANENT_UNDELIVERABLE_ERROR_CODES = {131026}` in `webhook.py`; every other code (including `131031` and any future unknown code) just logs, never blacklists the lead. Matches `upload.py`'s own existing `131026` mapping, so not a new convention.
- **Data cleanup**: one-time SQL cleared `whatsapp_undeliverable` on exactly the 56 already-wrongly-flagged leads (tenant `eba3ed94-277c-430f-a992-19bbe855e2f4`), scoped to leads with zero real `131026` failure on record. Dashboard/Segments counts matched exactly afterward for all 4 segments.
- **Three other real bugs found and fixed in the same investigation** (all in the frontend, all now fixed):
  1. **Shared Inbox phone search silently failed** on any query containing spaces — compared the space-formatted display value (`formatPhone()`, e.g. `+91 80568 95984`) against the raw digits-only stored phone (`+918056895984`). Fix: falls back to a digits-only comparison (`conversation-list.tsx`).
  2. **Dashboard segment tiles always opened the same tab** — `/dashboard/leads?segment=D` was clicked but `leads/page.tsx` (server component) never read `searchParams` at all; it always picked "the first of A/B/C/D that has any leads." Fix: reads `searchParams.segment` first, falls back to the old logic only when absent.
  3. **No click-through from Segments table to Conversations** — rows had no `onClick`; the Conversations page already supported deep-linking via `?lead=<id>` (unused). Fix: row click navigates there, `AssignButton`/edit controls stop propagation so they still work standalone.
- **Root cause of the count mismatch itself (separate from the bug above, this part is by design)**: Dashboard/Segments/Conversations count **three genuinely different populations** — Dashboard counts every non-deleted lead; Segments additionally excludes `opted_out`/`whatsapp_undeliverable`; Conversations only shows leads with real message activity **and was hard-capped at 50 most-recent, no pagination**, so older/cold leads silently fell off the page. Added real offset-based "Load more" pagination to the Shared Inbox (`conversations/page.tsx`, `conversation-list.tsx`) so it's no longer capped — this doesn't unify the three counts (they still answer different questions) but stops the Conversations number from being an artifact of pagination on top of that.
- **Files**: `backend/app/routes/webhook.py`, `frontend/components/conversation-list.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/leads/page.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/leads/LeadsClient.tsx`, `frontend/components/MobileRecord.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/conversations/page.tsx`.
- **Verification**: `backend/tests/test_delivery_error_classification.py` rewritten to the new API (4/4 passing), full backend suite 600/600, frontend `typecheck`/`lint` clean. Deployed and confirmed `live` on Render same day.
- **Still open (Meta-side, not fixable in this codebase)**: the tenant's WhatsApp Business Account is still restricted by Meta as of this entry — no outbound (including AI replies) will work until Meta lifts it. See active-backlog.md.

---

**2026-07-26 — Removed time-based decay from lead scoring entirely (explicit user decision)**
- **Decision**: composite score changed from `arc + intent_delta + engagement + decay` to `arc + intent_delta + engagement` — no decay term. Deleted `_compute_decay()`, the 6-hourly `apply_engagement_decay_all` APScheduler job (and its `main.py` registration/wrapper), and `score_engagement_delta` writes.
- **Rationale**: user's explicit instruction — "let the lead stay in same segment even if it stops messaging." A lead's score/segment must only move on something they actually said; the old bidirectional decay (−4 stale over 30d+ ... +3 for replying within 1h) meant two leads sending the *identical* reply to the *same broadcast* could land in different segments purely because one replied faster than the other. User confirmed the strongest option when asked: not "soften the penalty," but "silence has zero effect at all" — so the now-pointless scheduler job was deleted outright rather than left running or disabled.
- **`score_engagement_delta` DB column intentionally left in place** — historical values are now just inert; no migration to drop it, out of scope for a behavior fix.
- **Stale note corrected same session**: `subsystem-notes.md`'s Scoring section previously claimed `compute_score(broadcast_context={broadcast_id,broadcast_sent_at,tag_id})` — verified false by reading the live function signature; no `broadcast_context` parameter exists on `compute_score()` today. Corrected in subsystem-notes.md; see that file for what's actually still true about `broadcast_lead_scores`.
- **Verification**: full backend suite 600/600 → still 600/600 after removal (37/37 in `test_scoring_engine.py`, `TestDecay` class deleted, `TestCompositeScoreLogic` rewritten without the decay parameter, added `test_silence_does_not_change_score` as a direct regression guard for the new rule). Repo-wide grep confirmed zero remaining references to any removed symbol. Committed `0ea0747`, pushed, confirmed deploying on Render.

**2026-07-29 — Escalations "Reply" and Shared Inbox search both broken by the same page-cap pattern (follow-up to the 2026-07-25 pagination fix, this time the actual bugs)**
- **Context**: user reported clicking "Reply" on an Escalation for `+917824937422` did nothing, and searching that number in the Shared Inbox found no chat room. The 2026-07-25 entry above added "Load more" pagination but didn't touch these two specific paths, which were both still silently scoped to whatever page was already loaded.
- **Root cause 1 (Reply/deep-link)**: `handleEscalationReply`/the `?lead=` deep-link effect in `conversations/page.tsx` stashed the target lead id and waited for a `useEffect` to find it inside `leads` — populated only by `fetchConversations(folder, 50, 0)` ordered pinned-first then `last_reply_at desc` (per `get_conversation_leads` RPC). Any escalated lead outside the top 50 most-recently-active conversations never matched, so `setSelected` never fired and the click was a silent no-op.
- **Root cause 2 (Search)**: `conversation-list.tsx`'s search box filtered only the same in-memory `leads` array; `/api/v1/conversations` had no search parameter at all, so a conversation outside the loaded page was invisible to search too, regardless of query correctness.
- **Fix 1**: added `selectLeadById()` in `conversations/page.tsx` — checks the loaded list first, falls back to `api.leads.get(leadId)` (existing `GET /api/v1/leads/{id}`) and selects it directly, independent of pagination. Used by both the Reply handler and the deep-link effect; deleted the now-dead `pendingReplyRef` polling pattern.
- **Fix 2**: added `p_search` to the `get_conversation_leads` RPC (migration `151_conversation_leads_search.sql`, ILIKE against `leads.name`/`phone`/`tg_username`, applied before the pinned/recency ordering and `limit`/`offset` — so it searches the full matching set, not a page of it) and a `q` query param on `GET /api/v1/conversations` (`conversations.py`). Search state was lifted from `ConversationList` up to `ConversationsPage` (`searchQuery`/`onSearchChange` props replace the component's local state) so typing (debounced 300ms) triggers a real backend fetch (`limit=200`, no offset) instead of only re-filtering whatever was already in memory.
- **Verification**: frontend `typecheck`/`lint` clean. Migration applied live to Supabase (`ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`) + `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'`; confirmed via direct SQL that `get_conversation_leads(tenant_id, 50, 0, '7824937422')` returns the correct `lead_id` for the reported phone number. Not yet deployed to Render (code changes only committed to the working tree at entry time — confirm deploy status before assuming this is live in production).
- **Files**: `frontend/app/dashboard/conversations/page.tsx`, `frontend/components/conversation-list.tsx`, `backend/app/routes/conversations.py`, `backend/supabase/migrations/151_conversation_leads_search.sql`.

**2026-07-29 — Fixed duplicate leads in Shared Inbox (migration 152, applied on top of the search migration above)**
- **Context**: separately, user reported the same contact appearing twice, byte-identical, in the Conversations (Shared Inbox) list. Initial hypothesis (before touching the live DB) was a race condition in the WhatsApp webhook's check-then-insert lead creation — wrong. Live SQL confirmed exactly one `leads` row for that phone number, and a table-wide `group by` scan found zero leads with duplicate phone numbers anywhere in the DB. The duplication was never a data problem — it was the API returning the same lead twice.
- **Root cause**: `get_conversation_leads()`'s `combined` CTE did `conversation_leads UNION pinned_leads`. `UNION` dedupes identical *whole rows*, not by `lead_id` — the `pinned_leads` branch always emits `NULL` `last_reply_at`/`last_message_content`, so a lead that was both pinned **and** had real conversation activity produced two distinct tuples for the same `lead_id` (one with real values, one all-`NULL`), and both survived the union. `conversations.py`'s `lead_ids` list has no dedup and loops appending the joined lead dict once per occurrence — so the fan-out surfaced as a visually perfect duplicate card (both list entries resolve through the same `lead_map`/`last_reply_map`, so even the "different" NULL row rendered with the real values by the time it reached the frontend).
- **Fix**: replaced the `UNION` with `pinned_leads FULL OUTER JOIN conversation_leads ON lead_id`, structurally guaranteed to be one row per `lead_id`. No other behavior changed (same search filter, ordering, `count(*) over ()` total). Migration 152 — numbered after 151 above since this entry's fix was investigated and applied independently, using `pg_get_functiondef` to pull the *actual* live function body (which already included the search migration's `p_search` param by the time this fix started) rather than working from a stale local copy, so the two fixes compose correctly. Left `conversations.py` as-is — no defensive dedup added there, since the RPC now can't produce duplicates by construction and the extra guard would be dead code.
- **Applied directly to the live Supabase project** (`ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`) via `apply_migration`, with explicit user go-ahead first (schema/function changes are gated). Verified post-fix: the specific lead from the report now returns exactly one row with correct `last_reply_at`/`last_message_content`; a repo-wide `cross join lateral get_conversation_leads(...)` per-tenant check found zero tenants where `get_conversation_leads` returns more rows than distinct `lead_id`s.
- **Also noticed, not fixed (separate, lower-priority bug)**: `backend/app/routes/calls.py:245-268` (the SIM-basic manual-call auto-create-lead path) inserts `payload.phone` completely unnormalized — no `+91`-style cleanup unlike every other lead-creation path (`webhook.py`, `upload.py`, `telecalling_upload.py`). Ruled out as the cause of *this* bug (that path sets `source: "manual"`, and the reported duplicate's `accountLabel` showed "WHATSAPP"), but it's a live latent risk for phone-format-mismatch duplicates via manual calling. See active-backlog.md.
- **Repo hygiene note**: this fix's commit and the search-migration entry above landed in two separate local sessions that both branched off the same point and both used migration number 151. Renumbered this fix's file to `152_fix_conversation_leads_duplicate_pinned.sql` post-merge to avoid the collision (the live DB itself is unaffected — Supabase tracks applied migrations by timestamp, not filename). The merge itself (commit `a08aff0`) was completed via the IDE with the literal `<<<<<<<`/`=======`/`>>>>>>>` conflict markers left uncommitted-but-committed in this file (i.e. never actually resolved) and already pushed to `origin/main` before this was caught — cleaned up in a follow-up commit. If you see stray conflict markers in any `.agents/` file again, it means a merge was accepted without resolving content; grep the whole repo for `^<<<<<<<` before trusting `git status` alone.
- **Files**: `backend/supabase/migrations/152_fix_conversation_leads_duplicate_pinned.sql`, `.agents/context/subsystem-notes.md` (Conversations UI section).

**2026-07-30 — Lead scoring ranges and Cold-floor initialization**
- **Decision**: Segment settings are displayed as non-overlapping score ranges, while persisting the existing `{A, B, C}` lower-bound configuration. Every newly created lead and every newly created per-broadcast score record starts at the tenant's configured Cold lower bound (for example, Cold `3–5` starts at score `3`).
- **Scope**: Applied to inbound WhatsApp/Instagram/Facebook/Telegram leads, manual leads, CSV and telecalling uploads, call-created leads, and immediate/scheduled broadcast score records. Explicit segment overrides remain available.
- **Invariant**: A scheduled broadcast opens a fresh `broadcast_lead_scores` record at the Cold floor but must not change the lead's global score/segment. A reply continues global scoring from that lead's prior state; sending a template alone is not a scoring signal.
- **Known gap**: The immediate bulk-send route still upserts existing lead rows with the Cold-floor score, unlike the scheduled executor. Track and fix this before treating both send modes as behaviorally identical; see active backlog.
- **Verification**: Frontend typecheck, Python syntax checks, range mapping, and mocked Cold-floor initialization checks passed. The local backend venv executable denied access, so the focused pytest command could not run in this environment.
**2026-07-29 — Score History permanently empty (dead `decay` key crash) + lead arc-scoring and conversation compaction moved Groq → Gemini**
- **Context**: user spotted, from the operator console, that three leads sending the byte-identical Tanglish opening message ("En life la next enna nadakkum nu detailed ah therinjukanum.") landed at three different scores (6/7/8), and that every lead's "Score History" panel showed "No score events yet" despite the score clearly having changed. Traced live via Supabase queries, not assumed.
- **Root cause 1 (Score History always empty, since 2026-07-26)**: `record_stage_event()`'s metadata dict in `ai_reply.py` (both the main scored-reply path and the AI-disabled takeover path) still read `score_result["decay"]` / `_sr["decay"]` — a key `compute_score()` stopped returning when the decay term was deleted (see 2026-07-26 entry above). This raised a `KeyError` on **every single scoring event**, silently caught by the surrounding broad `except Exception`. `leads.score`/`segment` updated fine (that write happens earlier, inside `compute_score()`), but `record_stage_event()` — and everything after it in the same `try` block, including `maybe_assign_lead()` score-triggered auto-assignment — never ran. Confirmed via direct SQL: all three test leads had exactly one `lead_stage_events` row each, `event_type='created'`, zero `score_updated`/`segment_changed` rows, despite scores having moved from the seed value of 5.
- **Fix 1**: deleted the two dead `"decay": ...` lines. One-line fix per site, no other logic touched.
- **Root cause 2 (6 vs 7 vs 8 on identical text)**: two compounding causes, not one bug — (a) `_score_arc()`'s LLM call (Groq Llama 3.3 70B) returned 8 for one lead and 6 for two others on byte-identical input; Llama 3.3's officially supported languages don't include Tamil (same gap the 2026-07-18 `analyze_call` migration below was built to fix — this file was missed from that migration). (b) The three leads all shared the same `ad_campaign_id` ("Chat Astro Tamil" WhatsApp CTWA creative) — the "identical message" was Meta's own click-to-WhatsApp pre-filled CTA text, not something any lead composed, which the arc-scoring LLM had no way to know and could over-credit as composed intent (the rubric's own "first contact = neutral 5-6" carve-out should have applied but the LLM inconsistently over-weighted the ad copy's word "detailed").
- **Fix 2a**: `scoring_engine.py`'s `_score_arc()` moved from Groq `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` to **Gemini `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`** (`gemini_chat_completion`), matching the 2026-07-18 `analyze_call` decision. Same fallback behavior preserved (API/parse failure → return `current_arc`, not a hardcoded default) — a raise inside `gemini_chat_completion` (e.g. missing tenant `gemini_api_key`) is still caught by `_score_arc`'s own try/except.
- **Fix 2b**: new `messages.via_ad_referral boolean not null default false` column (migration `150_messages_ad_referral_flag.sql`, applied live to Supabase `ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`). `webhook.py` stamps it `true` on the inbound insert when Meta's webhook payload carries `referral.source_type == "ad"` (the same signal already used for CTWA ad-campaign attribution, just not previously persisted per-message). `compute_score()`'s transcript-builder now prefixes those lines with `[ad-prefilled entry message]`, and `_score_arc`'s prompt explicitly instructs the LLM to treat that marker as neutral first-contact only, never as composed intent — even if the ad copy contains intent-sounding words.
- **Also moved (same session, same rationale)**: `conversation_compactor.py` (rolling WhatsApp thread summarization, feeds `ai_reply.py`'s reply-generation context and `context_builder.py`) — Groq `llama-3.1-8b-instant` → Gemini `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`. Bonus fix as a side effect: the old Groq call was a **synchronous** SDK call inside an `async def` function (blocking the event loop during every compaction); `gemini_chat_completion` is properly async, so this is fixed too, not just the language gap. Compaction only fires once per 10 inbound messages (counter resets after each run), so the latency exposure of the provider swap is diluted 10x — not treated as a blocking concern.
- **Verification**: full backend suite 643/643 passing after every edit. Confirmed live via SQL that the tenant (`eba3ed94-277c-430f-a992-19bbe855e2f4`) already has both `groq_api_key` and `gemini_api_key` configured — no new credential setup needed. `via_ad_referral` column confirmed present on the live `messages` table post-migration. Committed `22979bc`, pushed to `origin/main`; pre-commit (`tenant-audit`, `backend-syntax`) and pre-push (`full-typecheck`) hooks passed.
- **Still open**: `conversation_compactor.py` has zero test coverage (no `test_conversation_compactor.py` file exists) — not created this session, flagged not fixed. See active-backlog.md.
- **Files**: `backend/app/services/scoring_engine.py`, `backend/app/services/conversation_compactor.py`, `backend/app/services/ai_reply.py`, `backend/app/routes/webhook.py`, `backend/supabase/migrations/150_messages_ad_referral_flag.sql`.

**2026-07-29 — Arc-scoring `max_tokens=4` silently truncating every Gemini call, plus handover-frustration and escalation-reply follow-ups (commit `8f818d77`)**
- **Context**: continuing the same day's Groq→Gemini scoring migration above, user flagged two leads whose scores looked stuck/wrong: one pinned at score 2 despite legitimate follow-up questions, another getting an "office hours" reply when asking about availability instead of being told the app is 24/7.
- **Root cause 1 (the real bug, bigger than either reported symptom)**: `_score_arc()`'s Gemini call still used `max_tokens=4`, carried over unchanged from the Groq code in the migration above. Live-tested against the real endpoint with the actual production prompt: `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` returns an **empty string** at `max_tokens=4` every time; `max_tokens=6+` returns the real digit reliably. Since the migration landed (`22979bca`, 2026-07-28 18:56 IST), **every arc-scoring call had been silently failing and falling back to whatever score existed before** — not a rubric/language quality issue, a truncated response budget. The `arc_updated`/`⚡arc` UI badge fires regardless of success or fallback, so this was invisible from the operator console.
- **Fix 1**: `max_tokens=4` → `max_tokens=8` in `_score_arc()`. Live-verified before/after on the stuck lead: empty-string fallback → real digit (`5`).
- **Root cause 2 (secondary, contributes to the "stuck score" symptom)**: the arc prompt scores the *whole* recent conversation arc, not just the latest message. A lead chasing an unresolved handover ("still no one contacted me", "when will they call") fills the 10-message window with frustration that reasonably reads as low-intent to the LLM, even when the lead's newest message is a genuine, unrelated product question.
- **Fix 2**: messages sent while inside a `chat_handovers` open window (`opened_at` minus a 5-minute pre-buffer — `opened_at` is stamped a few seconds after the triggering message — through `resolved_at` or now) get prefixed `[handover follow-up]` in the arc transcript, and the prompt is told this is response-time frustration, not product-interest signal. Live head-to-head test on the real stuck lead: marker applies correctly (confirmed in the transcript dump) but did not change the score in that specific case (5 either way) — flagged as unproven, not claimed as a confirmed fix.
- **Root cause 3 (separate lead, separate bug)**: `_escalation_prompt_block()` (`ai_reply.py`) permanently switches a lead's replies into "wait for the human team, cite office hours" mode the moment any handover is open, with no path back to mentioning the self-serve app — confirmed via `chat_handovers`: the affected lead's handover has been `pending`, unresolved, since 2026-07-25, four days before this was reported. Working as designed, but the design never gave the AI permission to offer the self-serve alternative while a handover sits unpicked.
- **Fix 3**: both branches of `_escalation_prompt_block`'s guidance now tell the AI it may also mention the self-serve app while the lead waits, instead of only ever repeating office hours.
- **Verification**: full backend suite 643/643 passing after every edit. Fix 1 live-verified via a direct `_score_arc()` call against the real Gemini endpoint (empty string → real digit). Fix 2 live-verified via transcript dump (marker correctly applied) but not verified to change any real score yet. Fix 3 not live-verified against a real reply (prompt-text change only, mirrors the existing verified pattern in the escalation block). Committed `94929a58`, rebased onto 16 intervening upstream commits (none touched the same files), pushed as `8f818d77`; pre-commit (`tenant-audit`, `backend-syntax`) and pre-push (`full-typecheck`) hooks passed.
- **Still open**: whether Fix 2 (handover marker) actually changes any real lead's score has not been observed — needs a case where escalation frustration genuinely dominates the arc read to confirm. The upstream handover-resolution-without-followup pattern that causes leads to get stuck chasing callbacks in the first place (see lead 82's 4 resolved-but-unaddressed handovers) is a process gap, not something fixable in scoring code.
- **Files**: `backend/app/services/scoring_engine.py`, `backend/app/services/ai_reply.py`.

- Reset owner password (Management tab) and single-client suspend/activate (Clients list + Management tab) fired immediately on click, unlike every other destructive action after the earlier ConfirmModal unification — a gap left over from that pass. **2026-07-29 — Operator: custom date range for Analytics "Daily Activity", scrollable Token/AI-Spend daily charts (commit `022637b`)**
- "Daily Activity" on the client Analytics tab was hardcoded server-side to the last 7 days (`range: str = "7d"` via the shared `_range_params()` helper) with no picker. Extended `GET /operator/clients/{id}/dashboard/daily-messages` to accept `range_days`/`all_time`/`from_date`/`to_date` — the same contract `get_client_token_usage`/`get_fleet_token_usage` already use. Deliberately did **not** touch `_range_params()` itself (3 other tenant-facing analytics endpoints depend on it) to avoid changing production client-dashboard behavior; the new endpoint computes its own date-window logic inline instead.
- Also fixed: Token Consumption (`TokenUsageCard.tsx`) and AI Spend (`ai-spend/page.tsx`) daily bar charts used `ResponsiveContainer width="100%"`, which squeezed every day into the fixed card width instead of scrolling on wide ranges (All-time/90-day) — bars became unreadable slivers. Fixed with a 28px-minimum-width-per-day horizontal-scroll wrapper (`minWidth` scales with day count, `width:100%` otherwise, so short ranges render identically to before).
- `DayStrip` (the client Analytics day-selector strip) already scrolled fine for any day count — no change needed there, only the backend contract and the picker UI.
- User explicitly declined extracting a shared `RangePicker` component even though the picker JSX is now duplicated 3× (Token Consumption, AI Spend, Analytics) — prefers matching the existing copy-paste pattern over a DRY refactor here.

**2026-07-29 — Dashboard fix: lead-source count/percent wrapping onto two lines (commit `22902af`)**

Fixed with `shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap`.

**2026-07-30 — Inbound Ad Performance is account-scoped and Click-to-WhatsApp-only (migrations 153 + 154)**
- **Decision**: Inbound Leads → Ad Performance reports only single-destination Click-to-WhatsApp ads from the tenant's currently configured Meta Ads account. Historical rows from previously connected ad accounts and Website/App/Messenger/Instagram-DM campaigns are excluded. This restriction applies only to the Ad Performance tab; the Leads tab continues to include WhatsApp, Instagram, Facebook, and Telegram inbound leads.
- **Data isolation**: `meta_ad_account_id` and `is_click_to_whatsapp` were added/backfilled across the Meta Ads reporting records. Sync fetches ad-set metadata, persists only WhatsApp-destination insight rows, scopes campaigns/creatives/insights to the configured account, and records last-sync metadata. Ad-set budget fields were added in migration 154 so reporting can show the actual budget owner.
- **UI**: Meta Ads credentials moved out of the Facebook/WhatsApp card into a separate Meta Ads settings card. The default performance table is WhatsApp-focused and customizable; Budget, Hot, and Cost/Hot remain available. KPI cards are WhatsApp Clicks, Aira-confirmed Messages, No Message, and Message Rate; Meta-reported conversations remain optional for comparison. The CTWA scope notice auto-hides after ten seconds. KPI descriptions were added without changing the established card dimensions.
- **Known semantic limit**: Aira-confirmed Messages currently counts unique `leads` grouped by the lead-level `attributed_ad_creative_id`, and date filtering uses `leads.created_at`. A returning person who later clicks another ad is not credited as a new interaction for the later creative because creative attribution is intentionally not overwritten. `No Message = inline_link_clicks - attributed unique leads`, so it can include genuine non-senders, missing referral attribution, and webhook failures. This is documented as an open redesign rather than treated as exact send-event accounting.

**2026-07-31 — employee_todos.tenant_id backfill (migration 162); telecaller-seat & numbers-pool enforcement restored; dead `/team/invite` removed**
- **Decision**: `employee_todos` (migration 027) was created without a `tenant_id` column, but `routes/todos.py` filtered/inserted on `tenant_id` everywhere — every `/api/v1/todos` call 500'd in production. Migration 162 adds the column and backfills it from `tenant_users`, same pattern migration 114 used for `conversations.tenant_id`.
- **Decision**: Reversed the 2026-07-05 "unlimited phase" for `telecaller_seats` and `numbers_pool` specifically (all other flat/metered modules — AI tokens, messaging, telecalling — are unaffected). `rbac.py` `create_user`/`update_user` and `numbers.py` `create_phone_number` now hard-block once the live count (`callers` where `active=true`; `phone_numbers` row count) reaches the tenant's purchased quantity for that feature. Both counts are live, so deleting a telecaller or number immediately frees a slot.
- **Decision**: Deleted the dead `POST /team/invite` endpoint (`team.py`) and its frontend `api.team.invite` wrapper. Telecaller creation now lives exclusively on the Roles page (`POST /api/v1/rbac/users`); the Teams-page invite flow had no frontend caller left and, being unguarded, was a bypass of the new seat cap.
- **Frontend**: `GET /rbac/users` returns `telecaller_seats: {limit, used}`, `GET /rbac/roles` tags each role `is_telecaller`, `GET /numbers/` returns `numbers_pool: {limit, used}`. Roles page "Create User" and Numbers page "Add Number" disable with an inline message at the cap.
- See `.agents/context/subsystem-notes.md` "Telecaller seats & numbers pool: hard enforcement restored" for the full addendum.

**2026-07-31 — Revoked-tenant session UX + To-Do input CSS fixes (same-day follow-up)**
- **Decision**: `AuthRoleContext.tsx` now signs the user out and redirects to `/login?reason=removed` when `GET /team/me` 403s with `"No tenant associated with this account."`, instead of showing a dead-end "Retry" screen. This is the expected outcome when a still-logged-in browser's user was deleted (e.g. via Roles page `deleteUser`) — retrying can never succeed since the account has no tenant. Root-caused from a live "Workspace Access Failed" screenshot; other `bootstrapError` cases (network blips, cold start, suspended account) are unaffected.
- **Fix**: `CalendarPanel.tsx`'s To-Do add-input had two CSS bugs — a native black focus outline showing through (`border-none` without `outline-none`) and its `+` submit button visually off-center (a `relative`-wrapper-around-a-bare-`<input>` baseline-strut issue, fixed by making the wrapper `flex`). See subsystem-notes.md "Frontend CSS gotcha" for the reusable pattern to watch for elsewhere.
- Also marked two active-backlog.md items resolved as side effects of the `/team/invite` deletion above: the generic "define future packaged limits" item (telecaller seats/numbers now enforced; AI tokens still open) and "Telecaller invite provider-aware fields" (moot — the Teams invite form it referred to no longer exists; Roles' Create User form was already provider-aware). Flagged `api.team.remove` / `DELETE /team/{userId}` as likely the same kind of dead code, not removed (out of scope this session).

**2026-08-01 — Two seat-enforcement bugs fixed same day: owner double-counted, and no free calling-module seat**
- **Bug 1 — owner double-counted**: `_active_telecaller_count` was counting the tenant owner's own `callers` row (seeded free by `create_client` so owners can also take calls) against the purchased seat quota, letting a client create one more real telecaller than intended. Fixed by excluding the owner's `user_id` (looked up from `tenant_users`) from the count. Live-caught: a test tenant with 0 actually-purchased seats had still managed to create 2 telecallers before the fix.
- **Decision (Bug 2 — missing free baseline)**: clarified with the client — purchasing SIM Basic or TeleCMI calling includes 1 free telecaller seat as part of that module purchase, not as a separate paid add-on; only telecaller #2+ requires an explicit `telecaller_seats` top-up request/approval. `_telecaller_seat_limit()` now computes `1 (if telecalling_sim/telecalling_telecmi purchased) + get_purchased_quantity(telecaller_seats)`, replacing the bare `get_purchased_quantity()` call both `_check_telecaller_seat_available` and `GET /rbac/users`'s reported limit used. `numbers_pool` was explicitly NOT given an equivalent free baseline — not asked for.
- 3 new regression tests added (`test_calling_module_grants_one_free_seat_with_no_topup`, `test_calling_module_free_seat_blocked_after_first_use`, `test_calling_module_free_seat_plus_topup`); full suite 746/746.
- See subsystem-notes.md "Telecaller seats & numbers pool: hard enforcement restored" for both bugs' full detail.
- **Same-day follow-up**: applied the identical free-baseline pattern to `numbers_pool` per explicit client confirmation — purchasing inbound or outbound (WhatsApp) messaging includes 1 free phone number, additional numbers require a paid top-up. `numbers.py`'s new `_numbers_pool_limit()` mirrors `_telecaller_seat_limit()` exactly. 3 more regression tests added to `test_numbers_pool_enforcement.py`; full suite 749/749.

**2026-08-01 — Operator console: added Roles nav (read-only), Team/Roles auto-disable when nothing else is on**
- **Decision**: the operator client-detail sidebar had "Team" but no "Roles" entry, and "Team" had no dependency on anything — it stayed visible/enabled even when every other feature was toggled off for a fresh/inactive client. Presented two options (give Team/Roles their own independent on/off toggle, or auto-follow whether any product feature is active) and picked the latter: staff management is meaningless with nothing active to staff, and an independent toggle wouldn't map to any purchasable `feature_catalog` row (would just be one more thing an operator has to remember to flip per client, for no product reason). `sidebar.tsx` gained a `dependsOn: "any"` type (messaging OR telecalling on) applied to both Team and the new Roles entries, alongside the existing `"outbound"`/`"messaging"` dependency types.
- **Added**: `GET /api/v1/operator/clients/{tenant_id}/roles` (read-only — role list with permission counts + user list with assigned role name, reuses `app.services.rbac`'s `ensure_default_roles`/`normalize_permissions`/`is_telecaller_role`) and `views/roles.tsx` (`RolesView`, same visual pattern as the existing `TeamView`). Deliberately read-only, no create/edit/delete — actual role and user management stays exclusively on the client's own Roles page; this is operator visibility only, consistent with impersonation having been removed entirely (see earlier 2026-07-02 entry).
- 2 new backend tests (`test_operator_client_roles.py`); full suite 751/751. Frontend `tsc`/`next lint` clean.

**2026-08-01 — Client Roles page visual redesign (no behavior change)**
- **Decision**: user flagged the client-facing Roles page (`frontend/app/dashboard/roles/page.tsx`) as looking plain compared to sibling pages (Templates, Numbers) and asked for a design pass. Rather than invent new patterns, reused what those pages already establish: colorful stat tiles with icon badges (new header row — Roles Defined / Total Users / Telecaller Seats), icon-prefixed inputs (matching the page's own existing search-input pattern), and avatar-initial circles for users.
- **Real find, not just polish**: `AccessModule.group` (Overview/Messaging/Leads/AI/Reports/Admin/Team/Telecalling) was modeled in the data but never rendered — the permission matrix table listed all ~18 modules flat and ungrouped. Added sticky category sub-header rows to the table using this already-existing field; no new data needed.
- Also added: colored role/user avatars (hashed-by-id palette, `Phone` icon for telecaller-type roles, `Crown` for the owner), empty states for zero-roles-match-search and zero-users.
- Pure frontend visual change — no backend, no behavior change, no new tests needed. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `next lint` (caught and fixed a real lint error — a local variable named `module`, which Next.js's linter forbids), and a full `next build` (compiles every route regardless of auth, since the dev server's auth middleware blocked getting an authenticated screenshot — flagged honestly to the user rather than claiming a visual check that didn't happen).

**2026-08-01 — 5th reply_language_mode: "tanglish_escalate_tamil" (Tanglish-with-Tamil-lock), migration 163 (commit `24b30d58`)**
- **Decision**: Added a new operator-selectable `reply_language_mode` alongside the existing `mirror`/`tanglish`/`english`/`tamil` — replies stay in Tanglish by default but permanently switch to native Tamil script for the rest of the conversation once the lead sends one message in pure Tamil Unicode script. Distinct from the existing `mirror` mode's per-turn `_reply_script_mismatch()` regeneration check (2026-07-13) — this is a one-way ratchet (Tanglish → Tamil, never back), for tenants who want the AI to escalate formality/language once a lead shows they're comfortable in native script, not to mirror every turn.
- **Why persisted, not inferred**: the lock is stored on `leads.tamil_locked` (migration 163) rather than re-derived from chat history each turn, because `_recent_thread()` only feeds the LLM prompt the last 8 messages — a script switch further back than that would be invisible to a history-scan approach and the lock would silently reset.
- **Implementation**: `_resolve_tamil_lock()` (`ai_reply.py`) checks `lead_data["tamil_locked"]` first; if unset, runs `_dominant_script(message) == "ta"` (Unicode-block detection, NOT `_detect_lang()`'s keyword-based Tanglish detection) — deliberately script-only so a Tanglish message (Latin script, Tamil vocabulary) never falsely triggers the lock, only actual Tamil Unicode text does. On first Tamil-script message, persists `tamil_locked=True` and returns `"tamil"` for that reply immediately (same turn), not just future ones.
- **Verification**: unit tests only (`test_ai_reply_lang_detection.py`, mocked `db`) — covers not-locked/tanglish, first-Tamil-message/locks-and-returns-tamil, already-locked/no-db-write. **Not live-verified against a real lead conversation** — same category of open risk as the `_reply_script_mismatch()` work's "manual smoke test owed" note (2026-07-13/18 entries above); flag if a real tenant reports the lock not sticking or firing on a Tanglish-only message.
- **Files**: `backend/app/services/ai_reply.py`, `supabase/migrations/163_leads_tamil_locked.sql`, `backend/tests/test_ai_reply_lang_detection.py`, `frontend/app/operator/(console)/client/[id]/views/config.tsx` (5th dropdown option). Plan: `docs/plans/tanglish-tamil-lock-reply-mode.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history).
