**2026-08-01 — IST calendar-day windowing for analytics, and a client-driven Analytics page consolidation (commits `05814474`, `18f7c578`)**
- **Bug fixed**: `/analytics/messaging` and `/analytics/inbound` bucketed and windowed everything in UTC calendar days regardless of what the client displayed, so a lead created late evening IST could land in the wrong day's bucket versus `/compare` (which was already IST-aware). `_resolve_window()` (`analytics.py`) now takes an opt-in `calendar_timezone: "UTC" | "Asia/Kolkata"` param (query alias `timezone`); passing `Asia/Kolkata` applies the same `IST_OFFSET` half-open UTC bounds `/compare` already used, for both custom start/end and the `today`/`7d`/`30d` presets. `_bucket_inbound_leads()` re-keys each inbound lead's `created_at` to its IST calendar date before aggregation. UTC stays the default, so any caller that doesn't pass `timezone` is unaffected. The frontend (`page.tsx` `reportingQuery()`) now always sends `timezone=Asia/Kolkata`.
- **Removed the "Needs attention" stale-hot-leads panel** (Overview tab card + `GET /analytics/hot-leads-stale` + its frontend API client method/type + presentation helpers) entirely, at the client's request after seeing it in production — a full 10-row list all reading "no reply since last message" with no real per-lead signal. Deleted rather than fixed: its stale-hot-leads test, `attentionScopeLabel`/`attentionLeadHref` removed from `overviewPresentation.ts`, `staleHotLeads`/`StaleHotLead` removed from `lib/api.ts`. The underlying need (surface leads waiting too long for a reply) is still real and unmet — flagged in active-backlog.md, not rebuilt here.
- **Removed the "Qualified" derived metric** (Hot+Warm rate card and funnel step) from the Overview tab per client feedback that it added confusion without action value — it was never a distinct lead status, just `count(hot) + count(warm)` computed client-side in `overviewPresentation.ts`.
- **Merged the Overview tab into Compare, consolidated the shared date/comparison controls**: client observed Overview and Compare showed overlapping metrics through two independent `RangePicker`/`ComparisonPicker` instances (plus a third one duplicated again for Channels/Inbound), so switching tabs silently reset your date selection. `AnalyticsPage` (`page.tsx`) now owns `range` AND `comparison` state once, in one header block (Reporting Period hidden only for Templates; Compare With shown only for the merged Overview/Compare tab). `CompareTab` (`CompareTab.tsx`) became a controlled component (`range`/`comparison` as props, no local picker state) and absorbed Overview's one genuinely unique piece — the funnel (New leads → Hot → Converted) — via a new `buildFunnel()` helper in `overviewPresentation.ts` (replacing the deleted `buildOverviewPresentation`/KPI-card logic, since Compare's own stat cards and segment-mix chart already covered New leads/Conversions/Cost per lead/Reply time and the daily trend line better). Also dropped Inbound tab's "By Segment" card as a duplicate of Compare's segment-mix chart, keeping only its unique organic-vs-ad and by-channel views.
- **Caught via `/code-review` before push**: the merge had silently dropped CompareTab's "Export CSV" button (`api.analytics.exportCompareCsv`) as unrelated collateral damage — restored it, and cleaned up the resulting dead code (`Download`/`ComparePayload` unused imports, unused `onRangeChange`/`onComparisonChange` props) so `next lint` is clean. Lesson: a plan executed by a different model can complete the requested change while silently regressing something adjacent it never touched intentionally — always diff-review before pushing, not just typecheck/build.
- **Verification**: 12 backend tests for the IST windowing (`test_analytics_custom_range.py`), full backend suite 738/738 after the hot-leads-stale removal, `overviewPresentation.test.ts` (3/3 after the funnel-only rewrite), `tsc --noEmit`/`next lint`/`next build` all clean.

- **Reach correction**: daily sync matched Meta's 28,492 impressions, INR 954.04 spend, and 434 WhatsApp clicks for Astro Whatsapp 02, but added eight daily reach values (23,461) while Meta's 30-day campaign reach was 17,309 unique people. Reporting now requests Meta's aggregate ad-level reach for the selected date range. The direct request is best-effort so an outage does not hide all report data; it falls back to stored daily reach and logs the error.

**2026-08-02 — Lead Scoring: fixed (non-configurable) bands, Disqualified renamed to Not Interested, score 0 introduced (migration 164)**
- **Decision**: replaced the per-tenant configurable A/B/C thresholds (Settings → Lead Scoring card, `scoring_segment_thresholds` app_setting) with fixed global bands: Hot (A) 8-10, Warm (B) 4-7, Cold (C) 1-3, Not Interested (D) = 0 exactly. Segment code `D` unchanged; only its label changed everywhere (`Disqualified` → `Not Interested`, ~14 frontend/backend files). New leads still start at the Cold floor, now score 1 (was 5).
- **Score 0 is new** — previously `score` was hard-floored at 1 everywhere (`leads_score_check CHECK (score >= 1 AND score <= 10)`). Two producers can now write 0: the explicit-rejection override in `compute_score()` (`scoring_engine.py`, fires on `_REJECTION_PATTERNS` match — "not interested", "stop", "wrong number", etc., previously force-set `score:1`) and the general composite clamp (`max(0, min(10, arc+intent+engagement))`, was `max(1, ...)`) — user explicitly wanted both paths able to reach 0, not just the rejection override.
- **`segmentation.py` simplified**: `score_to_segment()` dropped its `thresholds` param, `parse_thresholds()` deleted entirely, the `get_setting("scoring_segment_thresholds", ...)` lookups in `scoring_engine.py`/`segmentation.py` removed. Settings card removed entirely (not made read-only) per user choice.
- **Migration 164** (`backend/supabase/migrations/164_fixed_lead_scoring_bands.sql`, applied live to `ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`): widened `leads_score_check` to `>= 0`, set `score=0` for all existing segment-D leads (were scored 1-4 under the old default), then recomputed `segment` for every other lead from its existing `score` against the new fixed bands — necessary because some tenants had customized thresholds that no longer apply (e.g. 2 leads were segment A at score 7, 3 were segment B at score 8, pre-migration). Result: A 45→46, B 168→275, C 109→2, D stayed 14 (all renumbered to score 0). The big mover was 107 leads jumping Cold→Warm (scored 4-6, previously Cold under this tenant's old custom thresholds).
- **Found and fixed in passing, unrelated**: `.agents/decisions/log.md` itself had unresolved `<<<<<<< HEAD` / `=======` / `>>>>>>>` conflict markers checked in around the 2026-08-01 entries (same failure mode the 2026-07-29 entry above already warned about) — both sides were real, non-conflicting entries; removed the markers and kept both.
- **Verification**: full backend suite 761/761 passing (updated `test_segmentation.py` for the fixed bands, `test_scoring_engine.py`'s local segmentation/composite stubs for the new floor of 0). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` and `next lint` both clean. Post-migration segment/score distribution verified live via SQL against production.
- **Files**: `backend/app/services/segmentation.py`, `backend/app/services/scoring_engine.py`, `backend/app/services/ai_reply.py` (dropped now-unused import), `backend/tests/test_segmentation.py`, `backend/tests/test_scoring_engine.py`, `backend/supabase/migrations/164_fixed_lead_scoring_bands.sql`, `frontend/app/dashboard/settings/page.tsx` (card removed) plus the 13 label-only files listed via grep for `Disqualified`.

**2026-08-04 — Dashboard app header fixed above scrolling page content**

**2026-08-06 — Fixed `reengagement-rules` N+1 query causing Render instance flapping**
- **Symptom**: after upgrading `aira-ai-5tfr` from Free to Starter, the service still repeatedly showed "Instance failed: HTTP health check failed (timed out after 5 seconds)" followed by "Service recovered," roughly once a minute.
- **Root cause**: `reengagement_service.py::process_due_reengagements` (the `reengagement-rules` APScheduler job, `main.py`, `interval minutes=1`) looped over every lead one at a time — one `reengagement_logs` existence-check round-trip to Supabase per lead, plus (on the broadcast branch) one more `leads` fetch per lead — before even applying the cheap in-memory `segment`/`source` filters. Render logs showed 100 sequential Supabase calls in 10 seconds during one tick. Every 1-minute tick re-saturated the single Starter instance (0.5 CPU) long enough to starve the `/health` check past its 5s timeout.
- **Fix**: added `_already_processed_lead_ids()` and `_fetch_leads_by_id()` helpers that batch these lookups via `.in_(...)` (chunked at 200 ids/query) instead of per-lead round-trips, and reordered both the broadcast and inbound branches to apply delay/segment/source filtering in-memory *before* touching the DB at all. Collapses O(leads) sequential round-trips per tick down to O(1) (2-4 batched queries per step). No behavior change — same filters, same send path, just batched. Full backend suite (753 tests) passing after the change.
- **Files**: `backend/app/services/reengagement_service.py`. Commit `177ffe87`, pushed to `main`, `autoDeploy: yes` confirmed live on `aira-ai-5tfr` same session — no manual redeploy trigger needed.
- **Lesson**: a health-check flap on a paid Render plan (no free-tier sleep to blame) means something in-process is starving the event loop/CPU on every scheduler tick — check `mcp__render__list_logs` around the failure timestamp for a burst pattern before assuming an infra/plan problem.

---

**2026-08-07 — Paid Expert Handoff: generic in-WhatsApp consultation + payment flow (new module)**
- **What it is**: a lead asking a personal question (e.g. astrology) gets offered a paid human-expert consultation, collects the expert's required details via LLM slot-filling (not a step-by-step form), pays in-chat via Razorpay, and the AI mutes for that lead once paid. Fully generic per tenant — fields, trigger, offer message, and fee are all `app_settings`-driven (key `expert_handoff_config`), configured from a new Settings panel. First real client: Astro Tamil (₹29 astrologer consultation).
- **Why LLM slot-filling instead of a step machine**: `feature/client-requirements`'s `booking_flow.py` (never merged — a 5-step `collecting_name → collecting_rasi → …` machine) was rejected as a base. It broke on ordinary conversation — a lead volunteering two fields in one message only got one captured, and an off-topic aside mid-flow caused it to lose track. New module (`expert_handoff.py`) runs a dedicated LLM extraction call per message instead, merging whatever fields it finds on top of what's already collected, in any order.
- **Explicitly not the `[COLLECT_DONE]` pattern**: this codebase already built and fully removed (migration 130, 2026-07-05 entries above) a mechanism where the model emitted a hidden JSON tag inside its normal customer-facing reply. This new detection/extraction runs as two **separate**, dedicated LLM calls (`gemini_chat_completion_json`), decoupled from `ai_reply.py`'s conversational prompt entirely — `ai_reply.py` was not touched.
- **New table**: `expert_handoff_sessions` (migration `168_expert_handoff_sessions.sql`, applied live to `ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`) — one row per lead per attempt, `status` walks `offer_pending → collecting → awaiting_confirmation → awaiting_payment → paid → resolved`. RLS enabled, no client policies (service-role-only, same pattern as `platform_defaults`).
- **Payment**: `payment_razorpay.py` ported unchanged from `feature/client-requirements` — it was already generic (tenant-scoped via `get_setting`, no astrology-specific fields). Its Razorpay `notes` payload uses the literal key `booking_id` regardless of caller; the new webhook route (`routes/expert_handoff.py`, `POST /api/v1/expert-handoff/razorpay-webhook`) reads that key to mean "session id" — not a naming bug, just an artifact of reusing the ported function as-is per design decision D9.
- **Mute mechanism**: reuses the existing `leads.ai_enabled` flag and its existing check in `generate_reply` (`ai_reply.py:1164`) — no new mute path. This is a deliberate **contrast** with chat escalation (`2026-07-19-escalation-whatsapp-alerts-design.md`, D6), which keeps the AI live during a handover on purpose; here silence is intentional because a paying customer's consultation shouldn't have the AI replying over it. v1 has no automatic un-mute (no astrologer-side signal exists yet — see below) — admin flips `ai_enabled` back manually once a consultation is done.
- **Explicitly out of scope**: pushing the paid session to Astrotamil's own astrologer dashboard and relaying the astrologer's reply back to WhatsApp. Blocked on an unknown — Astrotamil's dashboard integration surface (webhook receiver? REST API? nothing?) hasn't been established with the client. `expert_handoff_sessions.status = 'paid'` is the intended hand-off point a future adapter will consume against.
- **Live-tested (2026-08-07)**, real Gemini API against Astro Tamil's own configured `gemini_api_key` (tenant `eba3ed94-277c-430f-a992-19bbe855e2f4`), function calls only — no WhatsApp messages sent, no session rows written: detection correct on 4/4 cases across English/Tanglish/Tamil script; extraction correctly captured two fields from one message, did not drop an already-collected field on an off-topic aside mid-collection ("also is Friday good for a haircut?" while `name` was already set), and correctly layered a third field on top afterward. This is the exact failure mode that motivated rejecting the step-machine approach — confirmed fixed.
- **Not yet live-tested**: the full WhatsApp send/receive + real Razorpay payment-link click-through (requires a human sending real WhatsApp messages and completing a real payment — could not be done from an agent session). Do this before enabling `expert_handoff_config.enabled=true` on Astro Tamil for real leads.
- **Files**: `backend/supabase/migrations/168_expert_handoff_sessions.sql`, `backend/app/services/payment_razorpay.py` (ported), `backend/app/services/expert_handoff.py`, `backend/app/routes/expert_handoff.py`, `backend/app/routes/app_settings.py` (+`ExpertHandoffConfigUpdate`, GET/PATCH `/expert-handoff-config`), `backend/app/routes/webhook.py` (routes inbound messages through `route_expert_handoff` before `generate_reply`), `backend/app/main.py` (registers the new public webhook router), `frontend/app/dashboard/settings/ExpertHandoffConfigPanel.tsx` + `page.tsx`. Full backend suite green (833 passing; 2 pre-existing unrelated date-boundary failures in `test_analytics_overview.py`/`test_growth_ad_performance.py` confirmed present before this work via `git stash`). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` and `next lint` both clean on the new files.
- **Design/plan docs**: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-07-paid-expert-handoff-design.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-07-paid-expert-handoff.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history).

**2026-08-07 — Aira Sync APK: real distribution (signed release build, Supabase-hosted download, Roles-page setup UI, Aira palette restyle)**
- **Decision**: the Android "Aira Sync" companion app (SIM Basic call-log sync, see 2026-07-02 entry above) had never had a real distribution path — only a debug build sitting in `android/app/build/outputs/`, and sync-token minting (`POST /callers/{id}/sync-token`) had zero frontend, owner had to hit it by hand. Both fixed.
- **Signing**: generated a real release keystore (`android/keystore/aira-sync-release.jks`) — PKCS12, so store/key password are necessarily identical (keytool silently ignores a distinct `-keypass` for PKCS12, don't be surprised by that). Wired via `android/keystore.properties` (gitignored) read conditionally in `app/build.gradle.kts` — `assembleRelease` produces an unsigned APK if the properties file is absent, signs it if present. **This keystore is the app's permanent identity — losing it means every future update needs a fresh uninstall/reinstall on every device.** Told the user to back it up off this machine; I did not do that part myself (credential handling call, not mine to push to a third-party store unprompted).
- **Hygiene fix**: `android/local.properties` (SDK path, machine-specific) had been tracked in git this whole time — only ever held a placeholder, nothing leaked, but untracked it (`git rm --cached`), added `android/local.properties.example`, and extended root `.gitignore` with `android/local.properties`, `android/keystore.properties`, `android/**/*.jks`, `android/**/*.keystore`.
- **Distribution**: new public Supabase Storage bucket `app-releases` (migration `169_app_releases_bucket.sql` — public SELECT policy only, no client INSERT policy; uploads are service-role-only via a one-off script using `get_supabase()`, matching how `call-recordings`/`catalog-media` already work). Signed APK lives at `https://ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/app-releases/aira-sync.apk` — same URL every release, re-upload with `upsert: true` to ship an update, no frontend redeploy needed.
- **Roles page UI** (`frontend/app/dashboard/roles/page.tsx`, owner-only, gated on `callingProvider === "sim_basic"` — TeleCMI tenants never see this, Aira Sync is SIM-Basic-only): a persistent "Download APK" card, plus a per-telecaller-row button that mints a fresh sync token (confirms first — re-minting invalidates the caller's current token immediately, no grace period, `callers.sync_token` is a single overwritten column with no history) and shows Server URL + token together in a copy-able banner, shown once. **Deliberately NOT exposed on the telecaller's own side** — same precedent as temp-password issuance, owner mints and relays out-of-band during phone setup; revisit only if per-telecaller setup volume ever makes the relay step a real bottleneck.
- **Gotcha caught mid-session**: the *only* APK file that existed (`android/app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk`) was a stale artifact — built Jul 3 (one minute after the Phase 2 recording-upload commit `a4e90191`), never rebuilt after that feature was reverted Jul 5 (`067d09bd`). User had it installed and swore it was "the current version" — it was, in the sense that it was the only build that existed, but the build itself predated the revert by two days and nobody had regenerated it since. **Lesson: a build artifact's mtime is not proof it matches current source — check it against the relevant commit dates, especially after any revert, before trusting an installed app reflects `main`.**
- **UI restyle**: pulled exact hex values from `frontend/app/globals.css` (`--primary: #5b21b6`, `--bg: #faf8f5`, etc.) rather than approximating — cream/white cards, violet primary button, a live-switching green/red status pill for call-log permission (was static text before), locale-formatted last-synced timestamp (was raw `Date.toString()` — e.g. `Tue Jul 07 16:33:31 GMT+05:30 2026` — before). No Material Components dependency introduced; stayed on plain `Theme.AppCompat` + drawable selectors, matching the existing (deliberate, previously-fixed) dependency set.
- **Files**: `android/app/build.gradle.kts`, `android/keystore.properties`+`android/keystore/*.jks` (gitignored), `android/app/src/main/res/{layout,values,drawable}/*`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/aira/sync/MainActivity.kt`, `backend/supabase/migrations/169_app_releases_bucket.sql`, `frontend/app/dashboard/roles/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/api.ts` (+`api.callers.generateSyncToken`). Both debug and release builds verified (`apksigner verify`, v2 scheme, one signer) and confirmed to contain zero "Recordings"/"source folder" strings (i.e. actually match reverted `main`, not the old stale build). Backend suite 781/783 passing (2 pre-existing unrelated UTC/IST day-boundary flakes, same ones noted in the entry above). Frontend typecheck + lint clean.

**2026-08-07 — Fix: false "Meta credentials not configured" alert caused by uncached, unretried transient Supabase read failures in `get_setting`**
- **Trigger**: user got a "System API Failure — WhatsApp Cloud API send failed: Meta credentials not configured" notification for tenant Astro Tamil, but was certain the WhatsApp credentials were actually saved. Verified directly (not from code alone, per [[feedback_verify_before_diagnosing]] in memory): `app_settings` had a valid `meta_access_token` (202 chars) and `meta_phone_number_id` (16 chars) for that tenant, last updated 2026-07-24. Correlated the `app_notifications` row's timestamp (2026-08-06 14:39:30 UTC) against Render logs for the same second and found the real cause: `get_setting(meta_access_token, ...) DB read failed: Server disconnected`, immediately followed by the misleading "not configured" error.
- **Root cause**: `get_setting()` (`backend/app/config_dynamic.py`) wrapped its Supabase read in a bare `try/except Exception`, silently falling back to `None` on ANY failure — a transient network blip was indistinguishable from a genuinely-unset credential. Worse, that `None` got written into the 60s in-process cache, so even after Supabase recovered, the tenant's next several `get_setting` calls for that key kept getting the false "not set" answer until the cache entry expired.
- **Fix**: `get_setting` now (1) retries once immediately on a read exception before giving up — clears the vast majority of these transient drops silently, before the caller ever sees an error — and (2) only writes to `_CACHE` on a confirmed read; a failed read (both attempts) is never cached, so the very next call re-hits the DB instead of serving a stale false negative. The function's contract is unchanged (`Optional[str]`, never raises) — safe across all 51 call sites. Genuine misconfiguration (row never saved) still correctly returns `None`/fallback, so `_creds()` in `meta_cloud.py` still raises its "not configured" message when that's actually true.
- **Tests**: added `test_get_setting_retries_once_on_transient_failure_then_succeeds`, `test_get_setting_does_not_cache_a_failed_read`, `test_get_setting_caches_a_successful_read` to `backend/tests/test_config_dynamic.py`. Full backend suite: 784/786 passing (same 2 pre-existing UTC/IST day-boundary flakes as the entry above, confirmed unrelated by reproducing them on a stash of this change).
- **Note**: this fix landed in a commit titled "system fail" (b016564) that the user appears to have made directly, merged with a subsequent `pull --tags origin main`, ending up already on `origin/main` — consistent with the parallel-editing pattern in [[feedback_no_push_without_approval]]. Flagged to the user that the commit message doesn't describe the change, in case they want it visible more accurately in history later.

**2026-08-07 — Meta-account-driven numbers pool sync + quota locking (supersedes "hard enforcement restored" 2026-07-31 for the discovery/import path only)**
- **Decision**: `numbers_pool` was previously enforced only at manual creation time (`POST /api/v1/numbers` hard-blocks at quota). There was no way to bulk-import numbers already registered on a client's Meta WhatsApp Business Account (WABA) — "Sync from Meta" only refreshed quality/tier on numbers already added one-by-one. New `POST /api/v1/numbers/sync-from-meta` (tenant-scoped) calls Meta's `{waba_id}/phone_numbers` Graph edge and imports every number found, **regardless of the tenant's purchased quota** — numbers beyond quota land locked (visible, blurred in the UI, functionally inert) rather than being rejected. See design/plan for full detail: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-meta-numbers-pool-sync-locking-design.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-06-meta-numbers-pool-sync-locking.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history).
- **Locking rule** (`backend/app/services/numbers_pool.py::compute_unlocked_ids`, pure function, no DB column — computed live on every read/route): if a `role="primary"` number exists, it always wins a slot; the remaining `limit - 1` slots fill oldest-first by `created_at` among non-primary numbers. If **no primary exists yet** (a brand-new tenant's first sync can pull in several numbers at once with none chosen), **everything is locked** regardless of `limit` — oldest-first only ever fills slots *in addition to* a guaranteed primary slot, never on its own. This was a deliberate correction mid-design: a pure oldest-first rule would auto-activate an arbitrary number by Meta's response order instead of letting the client choose, and would also fail the "downgrade" case (client had 2 numbers, drops to 1 — the actual primary must stay active even if it isn't the oldest row).
- **Enforcement, three layers, all sharing the one `get_unlocked_number_ids(db, tenant_id)` helper**: `GET /api/v1/numbers/` reports `locked` per row; `PATCH /{id}` blocks `status="active"`/`paused_outbound=false` on a locked number (but **always** allows `role="primary"` — that's the unlock mechanism itself, swapping which number holds the slot); `outbound_router.get_best_number()` filters candidates by the same live-computed set, which is what makes a subscription downgrade take effect **immediately** on the next send, not just after a future manual re-sync.
- **Deliberately unchanged**: manual single-number `POST /api/v1/numbers` keeps its existing hard 400-at-quota block. `failover.py`'s quality-red auto-promotion may still promote a locked standby during an outage (resilience path, not a quota bypass — it legitimately wins the primary slot once promoted). Inbound messages to a locked number are received/logged completely normally — locking only ever excludes a number from being *selected to send from*.
- **Bug found and fixed mid-implementation, not pre-existing**: the plan's Task 6 draft assumed `numbers.py` already had a `_TIER_MAP` constant (it didn't — only `_QUALITY_MAP`/`_WARM_UP_MAX` existed) and assumed the WABA `phone_numbers` list edge's `quality_rating` uses the same `HIGH`/`MEDIUM`/`LOW` format as the existing single-number `sync_number_from_meta`/`get_number_quality` path's `_QUALITY_MAP`. Neither was true. Added `_TIER_MAP` locally, and added a **separate** `_WABA_QUALITY_MAP = {"GREEN": "green", "YELLOW": "yellow", "RED": "red"}` for the new bulk-sync path specifically, matching the format `webhook.py`'s `phone_number_quality_update` handler and `failover.py`'s `QUALITY_MAP` already rely on (confirmed by reading both). **Did not touch** the existing `_QUALITY_MAP` (HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW) used by the older single-number path — see active-backlog.md, this looks like it may itself be using the wrong Meta value format, but that's pre-existing and out of scope for this change.
- **Tests**: 30 new backend tests across 5 new files (`test_numbers_pool_locking.py`, `test_numbers_locking_routes.py`, `test_meta_cloud_list_waba_phone_numbers.py`, `test_numbers_sync_from_meta.py`, `test_outbound_router_locking.py`) plus the existing `test_numbers_pool_enforcement.py` unchanged/still passing. Full backend suite 783/783 on the actual merged `main` tree (re-verified after merge, not just on the branch). Frontend `typecheck`+`lint` clean.
- **Process note**: built in an isolated worktree (`worktree-meta-numbers-pool-sync-locking`, via the harness's native `EnterWorktree`/`ExitWorktree`, not raw `git worktree`) through the full brainstorming → spec → plan → TDD-per-task execution flow. Caught and fixed a real gap before merging: the Task 8 frontend commit was never made during inline execution — the merge initially went through with only the 7 backend commits, missing `frontend/app/dashboard/numbers/page.tsx`'s blur/lock UI entirely. Found by checking the merge diff's file list against what the plan actually specified, rather than trusting the todo list's "completed" markers — worth remembering as a real failure mode of task-by-task inline execution (a task can be *implemented* and *verified* in-session without the commit step ever actually running). Also found and fixed a corrupted global Python 3.13 site-packages environment on this machine (pydantic/pdfminer.six/lxml/python-pptx all had partial installs causing `ModuleNotFoundError`/`ImportError` on submodules) via `pip install --force-reinstall -r requirements.txt` — unrelated to this feature, blocking the very first baseline test run in the fresh worktree.

**2026-08-07 — "TeleCMI" rebranded to "Cloud Telephony" in user-facing copy only**
- **Decision**: on request to "change the telecmi name to Cloud Telephony everywhere," scoped it to display strings only (labels, placeholders, tooltips, toasts, confirmation dialogs, and the two `HTTPException` details in `calls.py` that surface to the frontend as error toasts). Internal code identifiers, file names (`telecmi_client.py`), config keys (`telecmi_secret`, `telecmi_agent_id`, etc.), the `"telecmi"` string literal used as the `calling_provider` enum value, and DB columns/migrations were deliberately left untouched.
- **Rationale**: TeleCMI is the actual third-party telephony vendor still being integrated under the hood (`backend/app/services/telecmi_client.py`, CDR/webhook routes in `calls.py`). Renaming internal identifiers or DB schema would be a much higher-risk refactor (touches applied migrations, webhook contracts, tests) for what was asked as a branding/copy change. User confirmed this scope explicitly when asked.
- **Files touched**: `frontend/components/AppHeader.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/roles/page.tsx`, `frontend/app/operator/(console)/client/[id]/views/config.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/settings/page.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/settings/TelecallingConfigPanel.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/telecalling/components/sections/LiveAgentStatus.tsx`, `frontend/app/dashboard/subscriptions/CartBuilder.tsx` (cart item title, was "Tele-CMI"), `backend/app/routes/calls.py`. Frontend `typecheck` + `lint` clean.
- **Left as-is on purpose**: code comments and a `console.error` log in `LiveAgentStatus.tsx` still say "TeleCMI" — not user-visible. If a future task extends this to internal identifiers or DB schema, treat it as a separate, higher-blast-radius change (new migration for any column rename, never edit applied ones).

**2026-08-08 — Telecaller Dialer 0-leads bug: default Telecaller RBAC role was missing `leads.view`**
- **Trigger**: user reported a telecaller (Prem, tenant "Astro Tamil") had leads visibly assigned in the DB and in Conversations, but the Dialer's Lead Queue showed 0 / "No leads assigned to you."
- **Root cause**: `DEFAULT_TELECALLER_PERMISSIONS` (`backend/app/services/rbac.py`) never included `leads.view`. `GET /api/v1/leads/?assigned_to=...` (what the Dialer's `loadQueue()` calls) is gated by `require_permission("leads.view")`, so every telecaller using the stock role got a silent 403 on that call. `CallerView.tsx`'s `loadQueue()` wrapped both API calls in one `try/catch` that only did `console.error` on failure — no toast, no visible error — so the 403 rendered as an ordinary, indistinguishable "0 leads" empty state. Conversations worked fine because it's gated by `conversations.view`, which the default role does have.
- **Fix**: added `leads.view` to `DEFAULT_TELECALLER_PERMISSIONS`, then backfilled it directly via SQL onto all 3 existing `tenant_roles` rows with `slug='telecaller'` across every tenant (the code default only affects newly-created roles, not ones already persisted to `app_settings`/`tenant_roles`). Also added a `toast.error` to `CallerView.tsx`'s catch block so a future permission/network failure on this page surfaces instead of silently rendering as "0 leads assigned."
- **Related but separate finding**: 5 orphaned `callers` rows were found in the same tenant during this investigation (test accounts `yuvaraj`/`kalai`/`theee`/`abi`/`hgyuu`, created within a 5-minute window 2026-07-31, `active:false`, zero leads/calls). Root cause: `callers.user_id`/`tenant_users.user_id` have no FK constraint back to Supabase `auth.users` (confirmed via `information_schema` — zero FK rows), so deleting an auth account directly (outside the app, e.g. via Supabase's own Auth dashboard) never cascades to either table. Cleaned up via direct hard-delete after confirming zero `leads`/`call_logs`/`chat_handovers` references — see the entry below, this also motivated fixing the underlying delete flow so it doesn't happen again.

**2026-08-08 — Telecaller deletion now hard-deletes the `callers` row + tenant-facing audit log added**
- **Decision**: `DELETE /api/v1/rbac/users/{user_id}` (the Roles → Users delete flow) previously soft-deactivated the `callers` row (`active=false`) while hard-deleting `tenant_users` and the Supabase auth account — the exact split that produces the orphaned-row pattern found above once the auth account is gone too. Now it hard-deletes `callers` outright. All 7 mutation endpoints in `rbac.py` (user create/update/delete/reset-password, role create/update/delete) now write to the existing `app_audit_logs` table via `record_audit_event()` (previously nothing in this file used it, despite the service already being live in `app_settings.py`/`operator.py`). New tenant-scoped `GET /api/v1/rbac/audit-log` endpoint (gated by `roles.view`/`roles.manage`) plus a third "Audit Log" tab on the Roles page (`AppHeader.tsx` segmented control, next to Roles/Users) surface it.
- **FK safety**: `callers.id` is referenced by `call_logs.caller_id`, `leads.assigned_to`, `lead_notes.caller_id`, `follow_up_jobs.scheduled_by_caller_id` — all `ON DELETE SET NULL`, safe to delete through (history rows survive, lose caller-name attribution). `chat_handovers.assigned_to` is the one exception (`ON DELETE NO ACTION`, no cascade at all) — `delete_user` now nulls it explicitly before the `callers` delete, or the delete would 500. `caller_status_logs`/`caller_attendance_overrides` are `ON DELETE CASCADE` — those rows are deleted outright, not preserved.
- **Scope decision, confirmed with user during brainstorming**: hard-delete-when-safe (not unconditional, not kept-as-soft-delete) — accepted losing historical caller-name attribution on old call logs/leads in exchange for no more orphaned rows. Audit log scope is team + role/permission changes only, explicitly not "everything tenant-wide" (the operator console's own audit view already covers that superset for platform admins).
- **Bug found and fixed same day, post-ship**: the new `GET /rbac/audit-log` endpoint's query was copied from the operator console's `GET /clients/{tenant_id}/audit-logs` pattern verbatim, which has no `action` filter — appropriate there since platform admins are supposed to see everything for a tenant (settings changes, operator actions like `operator.leads_wiped`, etc.), but wrong for this tenant-facing page, which was explicitly scoped to team/role events only. User caught it by testing the live page (73 total entries shown, almost all `settings.updated`/`operator.*`, dated back to 2026-07-17). Fixed by adding `.or_("action.like.team.%,action.like.role.%")` to the query. **If any future `record_audit_event` call site is added elsewhere with an action prefix meant to be tenant-visible on this page, this filter needs updating too** — it's an allowlist, not a denylist, so new prefixes are invisible here by default.
- **`update_user`'s existing role-change path (`_ensure_caller_profile`) is unaffected and still soft-deactivates** — changing a user's role away from Telecaller (while keeping their `tenant_users` membership) still sets `callers.active=false`, not a hard delete. Only full user deletion was changed. Don't conflate the two when reading `subsystem-notes.md`'s "Client RBAC roles and user management" section — the "removing that permission deactivates the caller profile" line there is still accurate for the role-change case specifically.
- **Also found, deliberately left untouched** (see active-backlog.md): three other dead/parallel delete paths exist for callers/team members — `DELETE /api/v1/team/{user_id}` (unused by any frontend page), `DELETE /api/v1/callers/{caller_id}` (unused), and the operator console's own `DELETE /operator/clients/{tenant_id}/team/{caller_id}`. Unifying them is out of scope for this change.
- **Cleanup**: the 5 orphaned rows from the entry above were hard-deleted as part of this work, after re-confirming zero `leads`/`call_logs`/`chat_handovers` references immediately before deletion.
- **Tests**: `test_rbac_delete_audit.py` (hard-delete + handover null-out + audit write), `test_rbac_audit_log_endpoint.py` (tenant scoping + action-prefix filter). Full backend suite 834/834. Frontend `typecheck`+`lint` clean.

**2026-08-11 — WhatsApp Business App Coexistence: onboarding path (Approach A) + contact/history sync (Approach B)**
- **Decision**: added a second Embedded Signup path so a client can connect their existing WhatsApp Business **app** number to Aira while continuing to use the app on the phone — as opposed to the existing flow, which fully migrates the number to the Cloud API and away from the app. Two independent, sequentially-shipped pieces:
  - **Approach A** (live-tested with the pilot client, "Astrotamil Wa"): a second "Already using the WhatsApp Business app? Connect without switching" button (`ConnectChannelsPanel.tsx`) calls `FB.login()` with `extras: { featureType: 'whatsapp_business_app_onboarding', sessionInfoVersion: '3' }` — this is the actual switch that makes Meta's popup offer "connect a WhatsApp Business app" instead of only "create new WABA." The popup's finish event is `FINISH_WHATSAPP_BUSINESS_APP_ONBOARDING` (not `FINISH`), handled by a new `is_coexistence` branch that skips `register_phone_number` (the number's already registered via the app — re-registering is wrong for this path) and records a new `smb_message_echoes` webhook handler so messages the human sends **from the phone app** still land in Aira's `messages` table (dedup by `meta_message_id`, `is_ai_generated: False`).
  - **Approach B**: after a coexistence signup, `request_coexistence_sync()` (`meta_cloud.py`) fires two `POST /{phone_number_id}/smb_app_data` calls (`sync_type: smb_app_state_sync` and `history`) to backfill the client's existing phone contacts and pre-connection chat history. Handlers for both new webhook fields were added to `webhook.py`.
- **Product decision, confirmed with the client-facing owner**: unmatched contacts (phone-book entries with no existing Aira lead) and unmatched history threads are **never** turned into new leads — a phone contact isn't consent, and `leads.opt_in_source` gates broadcast eligibility (Hard Invariant 7). `smb_app_state_sync` only fills in a blank `leads.name` for an already-existing lead matched by phone; unmatched contacts and `action: "remove"` write nothing. `history` only backfills threads that already match an existing lead by phone; unmatched threads are skipped entirely, consistent with the contact-sync policy.
- **History backfill is informational only** — never runs through `scoring_engine`, `maybe_assign_lead`, or `record_stage_event`, so old conversations can't retroactively move a lead's segment or reassign it today. Direction (`inbound`/`outbound`) is read directly off Meta's payload (a `to` field present means the business sent it) rather than re-derived by comparing phone numbers. `created_at` is set explicitly to the message's real historical timestamp (verified `messages.created_at` has no trigger forcing `now()`), so backfilled messages sort correctly in the conversation view instead of all appearing "just now." Only `type: "text"` is handled for both `history` and `smb_message_echoes` — media (including Meta's `media_placeholder` stand-in for old media >14 days back) is logged and skipped, not imported; real scope, deliberately deferred.
- **Real-world onboarding friction found (not a code bug)**: the pilot client's number came back "not associated with the business you selected" mid-signup — the number was already claimed under a different Business Manager (a leftover from an unrelated prior setup). Meta's in-popup "unlink this phone number" link is itself broken (dead/expired-content page). Resolved manually via Business Settings → WhatsApp Accounts → find the number → disconnect. **Expect this on future coexistence onboardings too** — it's a Meta-side number-ownership conflict, not something Aira's code can detect or fix in advance.
- **Prerequisite work done in the same session, Meta-side only (no code)**: the `AIRA` Meta app was flipped from Development → **Published/Live** (was blocking any non-tester account from completing Embedded Signup at all, unrelated to coexistence specifically), and the coexistence-required webhook fields (`history`, `smb_app_state_sync`, `smb_message_echoes`) were subscribed in the Meta dashboard. `whatsapp_business_management`/`whatsapp_business_messaging` Advanced Access were already approved.
- **Files**: `frontend/app/dashboard/settings/ConnectChannelsPanel.tsx`, `backend/app/routes/app_settings.py` (`EmbeddedSignupRequest.is_coexistence`, `whatsapp_embedded_signup`), `backend/app/services/meta_cloud.py` (`request_coexistence_sync`), `backend/app/routes/webhook.py` (three new `elif` branches: `smb_message_echoes`, `smb_app_state_sync`, `history`). Specs: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-whatsapp-coexistence-onboarding-design.md`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-whatsapp-coexistence-sync-design.md`. Plans: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-whatsapp-coexistence-onboarding.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-11-whatsapp-coexistence-sync.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history).
- **Tests**: 5 new test files (`test_whatsapp_embedded_signup_coexistence.py`, `test_whatsapp_coexistence_echo_webhook.py`, `test_whatsapp_coexistence_sync_trigger.py`, `test_whatsapp_coexistence_contact_sync_webhook.py`, `test_whatsapp_coexistence_history_webhook.py`). Full backend suite 846/846.
- **Still open, not done this session**: the pilot client's coexistence connection predates the Approach B sync trigger (it only fires at signup time), so their contacts/history were never actually requested from Meta — needs a one-off manual trigger. See active-backlog.md.
- **Process note**: full brainstorming → design spec (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-telecaller-delete-audit-log-design.md`) → implementation plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-telecaller-delete-audit-log.md` (deleted 2026-10-25, in git history)) → inline execution flow, per user's "finish it" instruction skipping the subagent-vs-inline choice prompt and going straight to inline execution in-session.

**2026-08-11 — Removed WhatsApp broadcast auto-retry feature entirely (Migration 170, reverses 104)**
- **Decision**: Deleted the broadcast auto-retry feature end-to-end at user request: `services/broadcast_retry.py` (whole file), the `/broadcast/{id}/retry-now` and `/retry-timeline/{id}` routes and `retry_enabled`/`retry_time`/`retry_max_attempts` fields off `BulkSendRequest` in `upload.py`, the `"broadcast-retries"` APScheduler job + `_process_broadcast_retries()` in `main.py`, the operator console's scheduler-health row for it, and the frontend UI in `outbound-leads/page.tsx` (checkbox, `RetryTimeline` component, "Retry now" button). Migration 170 drops `retry_enabled`/`retry_time`/`retry_max_attempts`/`retry_of`/`retry_attempt`/`retry_completed_at` from `scheduled_broadcasts`, applied live to Supabase.
- **Scope confirmed via grilling session**: user explicitly scoped this to the WhatsApp broadcast retry feature only, not the unrelated telecalling "contact recycling" auto-retry (`services/contact_recycler.py`, `settings/TelecallingConfigPanel.tsx`, `recycle_enabled`) — that feature is untouched. Generic transient-error retry (`utils/db_retry.py`, `lib/api.ts` fetch-retry-on-5xx, page-level "Retry" buttons) is also unrelated infrastructure and untouched.
- **`broadcast_recipients.extra_cols` was NOT dropped despite originating in the same migration 104** — research initially assumed it was retry-only, but it's written on every broadcast send (`upload.py:727`) for template-variable personalization. Caught via file read before applying the migration; migration 170 only touches `scheduled_broadcasts` columns.
- **Also fixed while removing**: `GET /scheduled` in `upload.py` was still selecting and filtering on `retry_of` (`.is_("retry_of", "null")`) to hide retry-children from the pending-broadcasts list — would have 500'd once the column was dropped. Removed as part of this same change.
- **Pre-flight check**: queried `scheduled_broadcasts` for `retry_enabled=true AND retry_completed_at IS NULL` before dropping columns — 0 rows, no broadcast was mid retry-chain at removal time.
- **Historical data loss accepted**: dropping `retry_of`/`retry_attempt`/`retry_completed_at` erases which past broadcasts were retry-children of which and how many attempts each took. User confirmed this is fine, no archive taken.
- **Verification**: frontend `typecheck` clean, backend files `ast.parse`-valid, grep swept clean for all retry identifiers (excluding the unrelated `recycle_*` telecalling ones), live Supabase schema re-queried post-migration confirming the 6 columns are gone from `scheduled_broadcasts` and `extra_cols` is still present on `broadcast_recipients`.

## 2026-08-11/12 — Paid Expert Handoff: live-tested end to end, three bugs found and fixed

Live-tested the Paid Expert Handoff flow (feature described 2026-08-07) for the first time with real WhatsApp messages, real Razorpay live-mode keys, and a real ₹29 payment. Found and fixed:

- **`messages_reply_source_check` never had `'expert_handoff'` added.** `expert_handoff.py`'s `_send_and_log()` has inserted `reply_source='expert_handoff'` since the feature shipped (migration 168), but the live constraint only allowed `('knowledge', 'ai', 'automation', 'reengagement')` — every expert-handoff outbound log insert was silently throwing a `23514` violation. Uncaught inside `_send_and_log()`, it propagated out of `route_expert_handoff()`, was caught by `webhook.py`'s outer `try/except`, and since `consumed` was never returned `True`, the code fell through to **also** call `generate_reply()` — so leads got both the correct expert-handoff message AND an unrelated generic AI reply for the same inbound text. Migration 173 adds `'expert_handoff'` to the constraint. Applied live + verified.
- **AI was hard-muted post-payment (`ai_enabled=False`), risking silent leads.** If a lead asked "when will the astrologer contact me?" after paying, they got zero response until staff happened to see the `notify_pool` alert. Fixed by reusing the exact pattern the codebase already has for regular chat-handover escalations (`_escalation_prompt_block`): AI stays live, gets a new `_expert_handoff_paid_prompt_block()` injected (reassure, don't answer the original question, don't re-offer) whenever `get_paid_unresolved_session()` finds an unresolved paid session for the lead. Also added `'paid'` to `expert_handoff.py`'s `_ACTIVE_STATUSES` — without this, keeping the AI live risked `detect_expert_handoff_intent` re-triggering a **second** paid offer on the same lead while the first was still unresolved (a duplicate-charge risk that didn't exist while the AI was fully muted).
- **No way to close out a paid session.** `resolved`/`resolved_at` existed in the schema (migration 168) but nothing ever set them. Added `resolve_expert_handoff_session()` + `PATCH /expert-handoff/sessions/{id}/resolve` + a "Mark Resolved" button on `/dashboard/consultations`.

Also simplified `/dashboard/consultations`: dropped the full `ChatThread` (this page is for seeing who paid and what they submitted, not for in-line replying — the actual astrologer follow-up happens outside Aira), collected fields now render as a table. And fixed a real UI bug in `ExpertHandoffConfigPanel.tsx`'s fee input: it was a controlled `type="number"` that snapped back to `0` on every backspace (empty string → `Number("")` = `0` → re-render forced it back), making the field practically uneditable — switched to free-text with local state, `type="number"`'s spinner arrows removed.

**Tenant mix-up during testing**: this session's account has 3 tenants — `Astro Tamil` (`eba3ed94-277c-430f-a992-19bbe855e2f4`), `Astro Tamil - Co` (`9dfe3f53-a4f8-4c0e-99b5-853b42556ee8`), `Bloom Matrix` (`a069ee3a-81d5-49c6-b004-21c1a16276ac`). All the live testing above actually ran against **Astro Tamil - Co**, not `Astro Tamil` — the two names are close enough that early debugging (Razorpay creds, phone number, message history) was checked against the wrong tenant for a while before the user caught it via the operator Clients page. `Astro Tamil`'s Razorpay creds are still empty/deleted as of this session; only `Astro Tamil - Co` has real live keys configured. See subsystem-notes.md.

**Not yet fixed / explicitly deferred**: no Razorpay refund path exists anywhere in the codebase (checked `payment_razorpay.py` and the Razorpay MCP tool set — zero create-refund capability). No SLA/re-notify if staff ignores the `notify_pool` alert. Both flagged to user as next-priority if this goes to real client volume, not built this session.

Backend: 27/27 `test_expert_handoff.py` (4 new), full suite 864/864 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated date-boundary failures). Frontend `tsc`/`eslint` clean. Commits `9664bca1` (migration 173) and `697491c7` (AI-live fix + UI changes), both pushed to `origin/main`.

**2026-08-12 — Supabase + Render MCP wired locally; full infra-write autonomy granted for this repo**
- **Decision**: `.mcp.json` now has both `render` (http, project `tea-d7m36fsvikkc73fo8pcg`) and `supabase` (stdio, `@supabase/mcp-server-supabase`, project ref `ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`) servers. Both secrets moved to `${RENDER_API_KEY}` / `${SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN}` env-var expansion instead of literal tokens in the file (file was gitignored but had leaked a literal Render token into git history twice before, commit `f53e8052` fixed once, re-added at `3b464990`). User explicitly lifted the standing "ask before mutating live infra" rule for this project only — AI may apply Supabase migrations, run destructive SQL, and trigger/edit Render deploys and env vars without a confirmation step here. Applies to Aira AI only, not other projects.
- **Rationale**: User wants the AI operating this project end-to-end without a human-approval step for routine infra changes.

**2026-08-13 — Renamed Paid Expert Handoff to Intake, added tiered packages (Migrations 176, 177)**
- **Decision**: Full end-to-end rename from "Consultations"/"expert_handoff" to "Intake" — `expert_handoff_sessions` → `intake_sessions` (migration 176), `services/expert_handoff.py` → `services/intake.py`, `routes/expert_handoff.py` → `routes/intake.py`, `app_settings` key `expert_handoff_config` → `intake_config`, sidebar/more-menu/settings labels, frontend route `/dashboard/consultations` → `/dashboard/intake`. Driven by the same single-tenant-vocabulary mistake noted in the 2026-08-11/12 entry below repeating itself: the page name, the API prefix, and the messaging copy were all written assuming one astrology client. Settings tab "Messaging Channels" (holds WhatsApp, Telegram, Meta Ads, and Razorpay — not just messaging) renamed to "Integrations".
- **Legacy route alias kept live on purpose**: `/api/v1/expert-handoff` stays mounted alongside `/api/v1/intake` on the same routers (`main.py`) because Razorpay's dashboard has `/api/v1/expert-handoff/razorpay-webhook` registered externally as the live payment webhook URL — an atomic cutover isn't possible without breaking in-flight payment confirmations. Remove the two legacy `include_router` calls only after updating that URL in Razorpay's dashboard by hand. `reply_source='expert_handoff'` on `messages` rows was deliberately NOT renamed (would orphan historical rows for no visible gain).
- **Tiered packages replace the single `amount_paise` fee.** Config gains `packages: [{key, name, amount_paise, description}]`; a legacy single-fee config auto-migrates to one `{key: "standard", name: "Consultation"}` package (`normalize_packages()`), so the live astrology tenant's config kept working with zero manual migration. Lead picks a package (new status `awaiting_package_choice`) right after accepting the offer, before any fields are collected — chosen so the lead sees the price before investing effort in DOB/birthplace, and so per-package field sets are an additive change later if ever needed (deliberately not built this round — YAGNI, all packages share one field list for now).
- **Snapshotting, twice.** Both `field_schema` and `package_key`/`package_name`/`package_amount_paise` are written onto the session row at collection time, not read live from config at render time. Reason: field labels and package prices are both mutable and deletable in Settings — without a snapshot, renaming a field or repricing VIP would retroactively relabel/reprice every historical row, including already-paid ones. A blank cell for an old row that predates a new field is correct; a wrong label or a rewritten revenue figure is not.
- **Payment amount is recorded from the Razorpay webhook payload, never from config or the session's snapshotted price.** A lead can still pay a link that staff just superseded via a package change (the old Razorpay link stays live until Razorpay's own cancel propagates). `confirm_intake_payment()` takes `amount_paid_paise` from the webhook and sets `amount_mismatch=true` (new boolean column, migration 177) when it disagrees with `package_amount_paise`, surfaced as a ⚠ in the table and an explicit note in the drawer — rather than silently trusting the configured price.
- **`service_noun` config string** (default `"consultation"`) substituted into the three lead-facing strings that ignore AI tuning entirely: the WhatsApp payment receipt, the Razorpay payment-link description, and the `_intake_paid_prompt_block()` system-prompt section that steers (but doesn't dictate) the AI's wording post-payment. The package price list itself is rendered in Python from config, never generated by the LLM — a hallucinated price is a real liability the AI must not be allowed to introduce.
- **List/CSV replace the old bucket-only `/sessions` endpoint**, which silently capped at 50 rows with zero pagination (a live bug, not a hypothetical — confirmed via `.limit(50)` in the pre-rename code) and only ever showed `awaiting_payment`/`paid`, meaning `Mark Resolved` made a row vanish permanently with no way to audit it later. New endpoint: `status` (`all`/`awaiting_payment`/`paid`/`resolved`) + `package` + `q` (name/phone search) + keyset `cursor` (not offset — avoids duplicate/skipped rows as new sessions arrive mid-scroll) pagination, `limit` capped at 200. CSV export (`/sessions.csv`) ignores the frontend's column-picker (a viewing preference) but honours the active filter/search, capped at 5000 rows with a logged warning if hit.
- **Verification**: backend suite 898/902 passing pre-change baseline confirmed identical — the 4 failures (`test_analytics_compare_routes.py`, `test_analytics_overview.py`, `test_growth_ad_performance.py`, and this session's own `test_intake_csv.py::IntakeCsvRouteTests`) are pre-existing/environmental, not caused by this work: the CSV-streaming one reproduces identically on a clean `git stash` of this session's changes (a `sentry_sdk`/Starlette `BaseHTTPMiddleware` incompatibility with `StreamingResponse` under `TestClient`, unrelated to endpoint correctness), the two date-boundary ones are pre-existing flaky "today" comparisons in files this session never touched. Frontend `lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean. Live-queried `intake_sessions` post-migration to confirm all 4 existing rows backfilled correctly (`field_schema` populated from each tenant's live config, `package_key='standard'` with the correct historical `package_amount_paise` — ₹29/₹10 preserved per row, not overwritten with current config). Confirmed the legacy webhook alias is live (not 404) via local curl against both `/api/v1/expert-handoff/razorpay-webhook` and `/api/v1/intake/razorpay-webhook`.
- **Not yet done**: live WhatsApp run of the new package-choice flow and the field-drift table behavior — deferred to the user to run on their own phone against the live astrology tenant, since it requires configuring real (if low-value) test packages on production tenant config and sending real WhatsApp messages. Migrations 176/177 applied live via Supabase MCP with explicit per-migration confirmation from the user, despite the 2026-08-12 standing infra-write autonomy grant for this repo — asked anyway given the blast radius (table rename + status CHECK change on a table the live webhook writes to).

**2026-08-14/15 — Meta embedded signup rewritten onto granular scopes; Page made optional; verify token auto-provisioned**
- **Decision**: `discover_business_login_assets()` no longer reads `me/accounts`, `me/adaccounts` or `me/product_catalogs`. It reads the signup token's `granular_scopes` via `GET /debug_token` and fetches each granted asset by id. Commit `b764dcfa`.
- **Rationale (live-verified, not inferred)**: Facebook Login for Business issues a **system user** token. `debug_token` on a real signup token returned `type: SYSTEM_USER`, and `me/accounts` returned HTTP 200 with `{"data": []}` while the operator had demonstrably selected a Page and Meta's own review screen listed it. All three `me/*` edges fail (empty / `#100 Unsupported get request` / `#100 Missing Permission`). Every embedded signup in the product was therefore rejected with "No Facebook Page was granted", and because the Page check ran before anything was persisted, **the successful WhatsApp half of the flow was discarded with it**. `me/accounts` is kept as a non-raising best-effort merge so any Login configuration that still issues a user token keeps working.
- **Decision**: a Page-less grant now completes. `page_id` is optional on `UnifiedMetaSignupCompleteRequest`; without one the handler skips the Page claim, the Page webhook subscription and all `facebook_*`/`instagram_*` writes, and connects WhatsApp alone. Frontend picker labels the Page "(optional)" and stays actionable when zero Pages are granted.
- **Root cause of the empty grant is Meta-side configuration, NOT code.** The unified Login configuration `2026693308738446` requests WhatsApp permissions only; the asset picker shown in the signup window is driven by the configuration's *asset* setup and grants no authority. Confirmed on three separate signup tokens (17:52, 18:47, 19:03 on 2026-08-14) — all came back `scopes: whatsapp_business_management, whatsapp_business_messaging, public_profile`. Messenger/Instagram/Meta Ads cannot connect until page/IG/ads permissions are added to that configuration and granted Advanced Access. See active-backlog.md.
- **Decision**: `maybe_single().execute()` result is null-guarded at `app_settings.py`'s duplicate-phone check. Commit `f846bfaf`. Pre-existing bug, previously unreachable because the Page gate 400'd first; once the gate was relaxed it 500'd every first-time number (`AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'data'`, Render logs 18:47:29 and 18:48:05). Deploy `dep-d9vmbb942hec73alo04g` went live 19:01:05 and the next signup at 19:04:09 returned 200 — same flow, same inputs, fix was the only variable.
- **Decision**: `_save_shared_meta_app_credentials()` copies both `meta_app_secret` and `meta_webhook_verify_token` from env onto the tenant at connect time, on all four connect paths (WhatsApp embedded signup, unified signup, Facebook business login, Facebook embedded signup). Commit `e962cbcc`. Rationale: Meta never returns a verify token — it's a shared secret we choose — and `routes/facebook.py`/`routes/instagram.py` read it per-tenant with no env fallback, so Messenger/IG webhook challenges 403 until someone pastes it by hand (which is exactly what happened live at 19:06 on 2026-08-14). The app secret was already copied this way for the same reason; this folds three drifted copies of that write into one helper. **Deliberately overwrites a hand-entered per-tenant value** on re-connect: the embedded flows all run through Aira's own Meta app, so the env value is the one Meta will actually send.
- **Verification**: backend suite 1056 passing (5 new tests: granular-scope discovery against a faked `SYSTEM_USER` Graph, Page-less completion, first-time phone number, verify-token write). The 2 failures in `test_analytics_overview.py` / `test_growth_ad_performance.py` are pre-existing date-boundary flakes — confirmed by `git stash` + re-run on a clean tree, they fail identically without this session's changes. Frontend `tsc` and `next lint` clean. Live outcome: tenant `Astro Tamil` (`eba3ed94-...`) connected WhatsApp via embedded signup at 19:04 (`meta_access_token`, `meta_waba_id`, `meta_phone_number_id`, `meta_app_secret`, `meta_business_access_token` written; `phone_numbers` row `+91 73056 48808` created).

**2026-08-15 — Template lifecycle dates fixed; visibility fails closed on disconnect; 6 off-WABA rows purged live**
- **Trigger**: user submitted `escalation_alert` and the detail page showed "Created 24 July 2026 / Approved 5 August 2026" on a PENDING template created minutes earlier. Live `execute_sql` on the row confirmed it was the *old* row (same `id`, new `meta_template_id` `1383433297080515`, new WABA `1992670338047653`, July/August timestamps) — not a rendering bug.
- **Root cause**: `create_template`'s stale-row reuse path (`stale_existing_id`, added when WABA switching was introduced) UPDATEs the existing row instead of inserting, and `db_insert` never touched `submitted_at`/`approved_at`/`rejection_reason`. `submitted_at`'s `DEFAULT now()` only fires on INSERT. Same omission on the `PATCH /templates/{id}` resubmit path. Commit `002f7aa0`.
- **Three bugs found in the same pass, all in `templates.py`**: (1) Meta's literal `"NONE"` `rejected_reason` stored verbatim in all three ingest paths → red rejection banner on healthy templates (11 of 13 live rows affected); now normalised via `_clean_rejection_reason()`. (2) sync stamped `approved_at = now()` every run, so approval dates drifted to the last sync — four live rows shared the batch timestamp `2026-08-05 16:48:07`; now stamped once and cleared on non-approved status. (3) `sync_template_status` selected `name,meta_template_id` but `_belongs_to_current_waba` reads `meta_waba_id`, so the check saw `None` and **every linked template 404'd** — the "Sync Status" button could never have worked.
- **Decision — hide, don't delete, on disconnect.** User proposed purging templates and numbers when a client disconnects Meta. Rejected the delete half: `message_templates.variations` is local-only (written solely by `PUT /templates/{id}/variations`, read by `broadcast_executor`), and Meta cannot restore it on re-sync, so deletion loses config permanently. Instead `_filter_templates_for_waba` now fails closed (returns `[]` with no `meta_waba_id`), which produces the same visible outcome and reverses instantly on reconnect. Commit `a664254c`.
- **`_belongs_to_current_waba` deliberately keeps failing open** — it answers ownership, not visibility, and backs `create_template`'s duplicate-name check; flipping it would let an unconnected tenant insert same-name rows. Test pins this.
- **Numbers left alone at user's explicit instruction** ("don't touch the numbers just implement this for the templates"). The 2026-08-11 disconnect design already archives rather than deletes them (`status: archived`, `paused_outbound: true`) because call/message history references the row — so deletion was never the right move there either. `numbers.py` still *lists* archived rows to the UI; see active-backlog.md.
- **No draft concept exists in the product** — verified: zero live rows have a null `meta_template_id` or `meta_waba_id`, and `frontend/app/dashboard/templates/` has no connection-awareness. The local-save branch (`No meta_waba_id configured — saving template locally as PENDING`) was unreachable-by-design and is now a 400 matching `numbers.py`'s existing guard.
- **Live data changes** (Supabase `ayftynkgmfkaqmmnlmoc`, no migration — DML only): cleared 11 `rejection_reason = 'NONE'`; cleared `approved_at` on non-APPROVED rows; set `escalation_alert.submitted_at = now()`; **deleted 6 off-WABA rows for tenant Astro Tamil** (`hot_lead_check`, `whatsapp_notification`, `hello_world`, `escalation_alert_v2` on WABA `870961579116425`; `segment_escalation_alert`, `aira_ai_alert` on `1567090554797228`). Pre-checked: no FK references `message_templates`; `template_performance`/`template_performance_range` read `scheduled_broadcasts`, not this table, so history survived; the 9 broadcasts referencing `hot_lead_check` are all `done`/`cancelled`. Row JSON archived to the session scratchpad; the durable recovery path is reconnecting that WABA + "Sync from Meta".
- **Bloom Matrix's 5 rows were deliberately NOT deleted** — that tenant has no `meta_waba_id` at all, so under the old fail-open filter those templates were *visible* to them, not orphaned. The new filter hides them without data loss.
- **Verification**: 3 lifecycle tests + 3 visibility tests added to `test_templates.py` (12 passing), each confirmed failing before the fix. `test_waba_filter_keeps_all_rows_when_no_waba_configured` inverted to `..._hides_everything_...`. Full suite 1058 passing; the 2 `test_analytics_overview.py`/`test_growth_ad_performance.py` date-boundary failures are pre-existing — re-confirmed on a clean tree via `git stash`. Frontend `tsc` + `next lint` clean. Detail-page label "Created Date" → "Submitted Date" (it renders `submitted_at`, which now resets on resubmit).

**2026-08-18 — AstroTamil consultation bridge: ported onto Intake, follow-ups wired, silent-auth-failure closed (Migrations 179, 180; PRs #23/#24/#25)**
- **Decision**: PR #22 (Ansar Ibrahim, the astrobackmatrimony/Django developer) was **ported, not merged**. It was written against the pre-rename `expert_handoff` module; the 2026-08-13 rename meant git reported *modify/delete* on `routes/expert_handoff.py` and `services/expert_handoff.py`, so resolving the conflict markers would have restored the deleted module alongside `intake.py` — two live copies of the same feature. Work moved by hand onto `intake`; #22 closed, Ansar co-author on the merged commit. His branch `consultation-ansar-section` deliberately **kept** on the remote: the squash merges re-authored the files to `K Prem`, so that branch is now the only place the true authorship history survives.
- **Nothing that crosses the wire was renamed.** Endpoint paths, `X-API-Key`, `X-Astro-Signature`, payload keys and the `{session_id}::f{n}` ref format are a contract the Django side is already built against. The callback keeps its `/api/v1/expert-handoff/astro-reply` path — it works only because the 2026-08-13 legacy prefix alias is still mounted. **If that alias is ever removed, the bridge breaks silently on the reply path**, not at deploy time.
- **`confirm_intake_payment()` changed shape**: returns a dict (`phone`, `tenant_id`, `lead_id`, `customer_name`, `session`, `lead`) instead of a 4-tuple, because the bridge push needs the session and lead rows. Its UPDATE is now conditional (`.neq("status", "paid")`), making the read-then-write atomic so two concurrent Razorpay retries can't both confirm. **Deliberately `neq(paid)` rather than Ansar's `eq(awaiting_payment)`**: the looser filter preserves pre-bridge behaviour that money arriving against a cancelled or resolved session still surfaces to staff instead of vanishing.
- **Follow-ups (#24)**: `push_followup()` shipped in #22 with tests and **no caller** — a paid customer's second question was answered by the AI instead of the human they paid for. Now `forward_followup_to_astrologer()` fires from `route_intake` for every message once `astro_last_reply_id` is set, and consumes the turn. Free and uncapped, matching the Django side's ₹0 credit. **Not** muted via `ai_enabled` — that was the 2026-08-07..11 regression; instead the customer gets a composed receipt (`intake_copy` purpose `followup_sent`) and every failure path yields the turn back to the AI, because silence was the actual damage last time.
- **`astro_followup_count` (migration 180) exists because Django keys idempotency off `aira_{external_ref}`** and silently swallows a repeated `n`. Claimed with a compare-and-set so two messages arriving together can't take the same number, and **given back** on push failure rather than burned, which would leave a permanent gap in the astrologer's thread.
- **Silent auth failure closed (#25)**: a signature mismatch on `/astro-reply` returned 401 and wrote one log line — the astrologer's own UI still showed the reply as *sent*, and the paying customer never heard back. Nothing else surfaced it. Now alerts staff (`intake_bridge_auth_failed`), but **only after `external_ref` resolves to a real session** so the public endpoint can't be used to spam the tray, rate-limited to one alert per tenant per hour because Django retries, and alerting anyway if the cooldown lookup itself fails.
- **Settings UI added beyond #22's scope.** #22 allowlisted `astro_bridge_api_key`/`astro_bridge_secret` as secrets but gave them no input anywhere, so the integration could not be switched on. Added an `astro_bridge` channel to `connect-channels/channels.ts` (the `STANDALONE_CHANNELS` order assertion in `channels.test.ts` had to be updated with it).
- **Migrations 179 + 180 applied live via Supabase MCP**, verified by re-querying `information_schema` after each. Both additive and nullable/defaulted, applied ahead of the code merge.
- **Verification**: 1357 backend tests pass (36 new across the three PRs); all three PRs green on CI including Backend Tests. The two local failures (`test_analytics_compare_routes`, `test_intake_csv`) are the known `sentry_sdk`/Starlette `BaseHTTPMiddleware` + `StreamingResponse` incompatibility already recorded on 2026-08-13 — they pass in CI. **Nothing in this feature has ever run against a real Django instance**: `astro_bridge_url`/`api_key`/`secret` are unset for every tenant (live-checked, `app_settings` returns zero rows), so `push_consultation`/`push_followup` log "not configured" and skip.
- **Repo hygiene**: 20 stale remote branches deleted (all fully merged or with every commit already in `main` by patch-id; SHAs saved to a restore script first), and PRs #8/#12/#15/#16 auto-closed with them.
- **Still open, not deferred silently**: (1) credentials from Ansar — base URL, a `PermanentAPIKey` value, and a callback secret matching his `AIRA_BRIDGE_SECRET`; (2) whether his follow-up endpoint requires `customer_name` (we now send it per his own spec, previously omitted) and whether it has ever received a real request; (3) the package tier is **not** sent to the astrologer, who sees only a rupee amount. (A fourth item, Meta WABA restriction 131031, was listed here and is stale — the user confirmed on 2026-08-18 that it no longer applies.)

**2026-08-18 (later the same day) — Pipeline changed: the astrologer's answer redirects to the app instead of being delivered (Migration 181, PR #27)**
- **Decision**: the reading is read inside the AstroTamil app, so WhatsApp becomes only the payment counter and the doorbell. On the reply callback Aira sends one short nudge — "your answer is ready, open the app and sign in with this number" — and nothing else. `reply_text` is archived on `intake_sessions.astro_last_reply_text` for support; `reply_image_url`/`reply_voice_url` are ignored and the whole Meta media relay was deleted.
- **Deliberately NOT in `messages`.** That table means "what the customer received", and the answer is precisely what they did not receive. Support reads it off the session row instead.
- **Follow-up forwarding (#24, merged this morning) was removed the same day (#27)** along with `astro_followup_count` (migration 180 → dropped by 181). Verified zero rows carried a non-default count before dropping. Follow-ups happen in the app now. Roughly 90% of the bridge survived the pivot untouched — the push, the reconcile sweep, the signed callback, session resolution and the monotonic reply claim never cared what the last step sent.
- **The 24h window check and its staff alert were dropped on the user's explicit and twice-repeated instruction**, after the cost was stated: the window is Meta's rule on *our* number, so dropping the check doesn't remove the constraint, it removes our knowledge of it — a nudge that can't be sent means a paying customer never learns an answer exists. User's position is that the astrologer answers same-day in practice and anything later is the app's problem. **If that assumption breaks, the fix is a WhatsApp template for the nudge** (its text is fixed, so unlike a free-form reading it qualifies for template sending outside the window) rather than reinstating the check — `send_template_message()` already exists in `meta_cloud.py`.
- **Three messages had to change with it**: the payment receipt's LLM instruction, its English fallback, and `_intake_paid_prompt_block` all promised the expert would be "in touch here on WhatsApp". True until this change, a lie after it, and the receipt fires on every payment. The receipt now says the expert is working on it and we'll message when ready, and deliberately does **not** mention the app — telling someone to download it before their answer exists earns a bad review. `_intake_paid_prompt_block` gained `answer_in_app`, driven by whether the tenant has an `app_download_link`, so non-app tenants keep the old wording exactly.
- **Nothing had to be reverted for AI muting**: removing #24's `route_intake` branch restored the pre-#24 behaviour on its own, so a paid lead's questions reach `generate_reply` as before.
- **Every path that can write the app link is forbidden to the model.** Only `_compose_reply_nudge` appends it, from settings. A hallucinated download URL sent to a paying customer is the failure `24494b3d` already fixed once on the offer message.
- **Verification**: 1349 backend tests pass, 15 new/rewritten (the two reply-hardening tests that asserted *delivery* were rewritten for the nudge). Migration 181 applied live and re-queried. Same two known-environmental local failures; CI green.
- **Still open and unchanged**: credentials from Ansar, whether the app matches customers on the same phone format Aira pushes (`+91…` vs bare digits — the one remaining silent-failure path, and it fails on someone who has already paid), and the package tier still not being sent. (WABA restriction 131031 was cited here as a fourth blocker; the user confirmed the same day that it is stale and no longer applies.)

## 2026-08-19 — WhatsApp typing indicator (commit `49af8580`)
- **Decision**: fire Cloud API `typing_indicator` + a read receipt right before intake/reply routing, in `backend/app/services/meta_cloud.py` (new function) called from `webhook.py`. Best-effort — a failed call never blocks the actual send, since AI draft latency (several seconds through Groq) previously left the customer staring at an unread message with no feedback.
- **Files**: `backend/app/routes/webhook.py`, `backend/app/services/meta_cloud.py`.

## 2026-08-19 — Intake resolve button, double-reply bug, stale-session sweep; brain-led intake parked (commit `5341d57a`)
- **Context**: four fixes to the Paid Intake / AI reply path, found while auditing why AI replies had drifted since Razorpay/Intake landed.
- **Resolve button wired**: the Intake table's Resolve button now calls `PATCH /intake/sessions/{id}/resolve`, which has existed since 2026-08-12 but nothing in the frontend ever called it (see active-backlog.md, now closed).
- **Double-reply bug fixed — load-bearing gotcha**: `_send_and_log` in `backend/app/services/intake.py` sent the WhatsApp message *before* logging it. A logging failure (e.g. the `messages_reply_source_check` constraint) raised after the customer already had the message, so `route_intake` reported the turn unconsumed, and `generate_reply` then sent a second, unrelated reply on top of it. Logging failure is now non-fatal — log-then-send-fails is safe, send-then-log-fails duplicates. See `[[subsystem-notes]]` for the general pattern.
- **48h sweep added**: a 5-minute job (mirrors astro-push-reconcile) cancels `awaiting_payment` sessions older than 48h (frees the lead; a late payment is still honored since `confirm_intake_payment` doesn't gate on status) and auto-resolves `paid` sessions older than 48h the same way the manual button does. Supersedes the previously-agreed "auto-resolve on first human outbound reply" design (see 2026-08-15 entry) — the sweep was simpler to ship and covers the same stuck-forever failure mode.
- **Brain-led intake parked, not deleted**: `backend/app/services/intake_brain.py`, gated to one phone number (`intake_brain_led_phones` app_setting) since 2026-08-15, was never live-verified beyond that one number and was identified as the source of most of `generate_reply`'s added complexity. Stripped from `main`, preserved intact on branch `feature/brain-led-intake`. Composer-led `route_intake` is now the only live path on main.
- **Files**: `backend/app/main.py`, `backend/app/services/ai_reply.py`, `backend/app/services/intake.py`.

## 2026-08-22 — App-link/catalog/escalation leaks into non-intake replies; CSV export 500s (commit `5716c840`)
- **Context**: user-reported regression on the AstroTamil WhatsApp number (+91 80156 60288) — leads asking a vague/emotional question got a reflex app-link dump (4-paragraph reply) instead of a short, warm answer, even with Paid Intake disabled. Traced with a live DB check: `intake_config.enabled=false` on the tenant, yet the bug still fired — proved it wasn't intake-state-dependent at all.
- **`Astro Tamil` and `Astro Tamil - Co` are sister companies that intentionally share the same KB/master-prompt content, kept in sync by hand, not automatically.** Initially quoted `Astro Tamil`'s (`eba3ed94...`) elaborate 13,678-char master prompt as the evidence, from prompt richness alone. The number in the actual screenshot belongs to `Astro Tamil - Co` (`9dfe3f53...`) per `phone_numbers.number` — confirmed after the fact. Per the user (2026-08-22): the master prompt had been updated on `Astro Tamil` but the same update hadn't yet been copied over to `Astro Tamil - Co` — not a wrong-tenant debugging error, just the manual sync lagging between the two. `Astro Tamil - Co`'s prompt at the time (906 chars) was the pre-update version. The diagnosis and code fix below are unaffected either way — the injected block leaked for both tenants regardless of which prompt version each was running. Worth knowing for next time: these two tenants' KB/prompt content is expected to diverge whenever one is updated and the other isn't yet — check both before assuming they're identical.
- **Root cause — load-bearing gotcha**: `_build_base_prompt()` in `ai_reply.py` appended an unconditional `"Use this exact link whenever you need to share the app..."` directive whenever a tenant had `app_download_link` set (added by commit `35c590a2`, 2026-07-20, *before* Paid Intake existed — to stop the model inventing URLs). Paid Intake's astrologer-answer-redirect (`164f526c`, 2026-08-18) made that setting load-bearing for the first time on these tenants, which woke up a dormant block that had nothing to do with intake. Confirmed live: both `Astro Tamil` and `Astro Tamil - Co` had `app_download_link` set with `intake_config.enabled=false` at the time of the bug report — a global leak, not tenant-specific. Fix: reworded to reference-only text (the URL stays available so the model still never invents one, but nothing tells it to use it by default) — see `[[subsystem-notes]]`.
