**2026-09-09 — Knowledge Base: Description first, and documents gated behind it**
- **Decision**: The Description tab is now the Knowledge Base's default landing tab, and uploading RAG documents is blocked until both the business description and the lead scoring rubric are saved. The tab pills in `components/AppHeader.tsx` were reordered to `Description` → `Documents (RAG)`, the header title now defaults to `Knowledge Base / Description`, and the Description pill **deletes** the `?tab=` param (canonical URL stays `/dashboard/knowledge`) rather than writing `?tab=description` — same convention as the numbers switcher. `?tab=ai-tune` still resolves to Description; only an explicit `?tab=documents` opens the library.
- **Rationale**: the ordering mirrors how `_build_base_prompt()` actually assembles the prompt — master prompt, then `business_description` (injected in full on every reply), then retrieved knowledge excerpts. Excerpts arrive with the document name stripped and can miss entirely, so documents are only useful on top of an assistant that already knows who it is. Uploading first produces a knowledge base the AI cannot really use.
- **Scope of the gate**: uploading only. Existing documents stay listed, viewable, downloadable and deletable. The dropzone is replaced by a locked panel with a two-item checklist and a "Go to Description" button; drag-and-drop goes inert; `processUpload()` itself refuses with an error message so the gate holds even if a button is reached another way; the campaign-scope selector hides; the empty-state CTA switches to "Go to Description". Read-only roles see "Ask an account owner to complete these".
- **Live-tenant consequence, deliberate**: any existing tenant that has documents but no saved `scoring_rubric` will hit the lock on their next upload. Accepted at the user's direction.
- **Also**: the rubric auto-update switch was a hand-rolled track/knob and now uses the shared `SwitchPill` from `components/ui/controls.tsx` (loading spinner included), and `toggleRubricAutoUpdate` takes the next value as a parameter instead of deriving it from state. A plain-language guide card was added at the top of the Description tab (four numbered points with worked examples, keep-it-short / leave-out rules, and a collapsed copyable fill-in-the-blanks template) — written for business owners, not prompt engineers.
- **Files**: `frontend/app/dashboard/knowledge/page.tsx`, `frontend/components/AppHeader.tsx`.
- **Verification**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `next lint` 0 errors (7 pre-existing warnings elsewhere, unchanged). Both new surfaces rendered in Chrome via a temporary unguarded preview route and screenshotted — the real `/dashboard/knowledge` route needs a Supabase session that this harness does not have.

**2026-09-09 — Knowledge page: "what to upload" guide on Documents, and the Description guide rewritten around what nothing else supplies**
- **Decision**: Added a plain-language guide card to the **Documents (RAG)** tab mirroring the one shipped earlier the same day on Description — six numbered categories (prices/packages, what a service involves, everyday questions, policies, locations, proof of work), each with a worked example, plus keep/leave-out callouts and a collapsed copyable template (`RAG_TEMPLATE`). Placed above the stats cards.
- **Decision**: **Removed opening hours and reply language from the Description guide entirely.** Both are structured settings that `ai_reply` injects live — `business_hours` with real open/closed state, `reply_language_mode` via `_language_rule_block` — so asking a client to type them into the description creates a second copy that goes stale and then contradicts the live one mid-conversation. The "leave out" callout now names hours, language and app link explicitly.
- **Decision**: The three points that replaced them are the ones nothing else in the system can know — *what you want Aira to do* (the goal of the conversation), *what Aira must never do*, and *when to fetch a person*. Rationale: without the first, Aira answers politely and lets the lead go; without the other two it is a customer-care agent with no brakes. Service area folded into "Who you are". `DESCRIPTION_TEMPLATE` rewritten from one prose blob into headed sections (ABOUT US / WHAT WE OFFER / WHO WE TALK TO / YOUR JOB IN EVERY CONVERSATION / WHAT YOU MUST NEVER DO / HAND OVER TO A PERSON WHEN / HOW TO SOUND) with business-type-neutral placeholders; guidance moved 150–300 → 200–350 words.
- **Correction shipped same day**: the Documents callout first claimed "scans of handwriting and text inside images cannot be read at all". That was asserted without reading the pipeline and is **false** — `extract_text_from_file` OCRs fully-scanned PDFs and standalone images via Gemini. The real failure is narrower and silent: a PDF mixing real text with an image never triggers the fallback. Copy corrected to describe that case. See the backlog entry for the unfixed pipeline gap.
- **Files**: `frontend/app/dashboard/knowledge/page.tsx` (commits `1b509000`, `f6d78bf8`, `bd374303`).
- **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `next lint` clean on the file. Both panels rendered in Chrome at 1440px through a temporary unguarded preview route and screenshotted (the real route needs a Supabase session this harness lacks); fonts fell back to system faces because the box has no network.

## 2026-09-10 — Meta Ads "Delivery" column showed Active for ads deleted in Ads Manager (migration 189)

- **Symptom**: Two ads deleted in Ads Manager kept rendering as `Active` on the Meta Ads dashboard. Live evidence: their `ad_creatives` rows were last touched 2026-08-14 while sibling rows synced the same morning.
- **Root cause (three compounding)**:
  1. **No ad-level status existed anywhere.** `ad_creatives.effective_status` is written from `adset_meta` (the *ad set's* status), and `ad_performance.py` rendered `ad_campaigns.effective_status` (the *campaign's*). The sync never called the `/ads` edge at all.
  2. **The sync is update-only.** `sync_campaign_meta` / `upsert_creative_from_insight` walk what Meta *returns* and update matches. Meta hides DELETED/ARCHIVED objects unless a request names them, so a deleted ad simply stopped appearing and its last-known `ACTIVE` stuck forever. Nothing reconciled disappearances.
  3. **Campaigns were de-duplicated by name.** `get_or_create_campaign`'s name fallback adopted *any* same-named row and then overwrote its `external_campaign_id`, collapsing two distinct Meta campaigns named "Astro Whatsapp-WB" into one row — so the deleted ad inherited the still-live campaign's `ACTIVE`.
- **Decision**:
  - **Migration 189**: `ad_creatives.ad_effective_status` (the ad's own status) + `last_seen_at`. NULL on existing rows falls back to the old campaign/ad-set behaviour, so no backfill.
  - New `sync_ad_delivery_status()` runs after the creative upserts: pass 1 writes each ad's real status from the `/ads` edge (requested with an explicit `ad.effective_status IN [...]` filter so DELETED/ARCHIVED come back); pass 2 marks any row for that `(tenant, account)` which pass 1 did not touch as `DELETED`. **An empty `/ads` response skips pass 2** — an empty edge means a failed or permission-denied fetch far more often than an empty account, and mass-marking a tenant's table would be worse than the stale status being fixed.
  - `_fetch_adsets` now also requests deleted/archived, so a deleted ad's insight rows still classify as Click-to-WhatsApp in `_whatsapp_rows`.
  - The filtering syntax is version-sensitive, so a 400 falls back to an unfiltered fetch; pass 2 still catches the deletion, it just can't tell DELETED from ARCHIVED.
  - `get_or_create_campaign` now refuses a name match on a row already claimed by a *different* `external_campaign_id`. The name fallback itself stays — CSV upload supplies a name and no id and depends on it.
  - API/CSV field `campaign_status` renamed to **`delivery_status`** (it is no longer the campaign's), preferring `ad_effective_status` → campaign → ad set.
  - Frontend: `DELETED`/`ARCHIVED` render as a grey "Deleted" badge with a ban icon; the row dims to 55% with the creative name struck through, and restores to full opacity on hover. Deleted rows stay in the table and still count toward the Total — their spend is real.
- **Not verified**: the `/ads` edge `filtering` syntax was not exercised against the live Graph API (no network from the dev shell). The `last_seen_at` reconciliation is deliberately independent of it, so deletions are caught either way.

## 2026-09-11 — Meta Ads KPI cards scoped to live ads; Delivery filter added

- **Context**: Follow-up to the 2026-09-10 delivery-status fix. Once archived ads were labelled correctly, the four KPI cards were still summing them — 84 clicks headline when only 81 came from live ads.
- **Live confirmation of the previous fix**: Meta returned both removed ads with `effective_status = ARCHIVED` (not `DELETED`) and `last_seen_at` stamped on all four rows. So the `/ads` edge `filtering` syntax **is** verified against the live Graph API — the caveat in the 2026-09-10 entry is closed. Deleting an ad in Ads Manager archives it; Meta keeps ads that carry spend history.
- **Decision**: KPI cards exclude `ARCHIVED`/`DELETED` and carry a one-line note naming how many ads were left out; the table and its Total keep counting them, because archived ads spent real money and hiding it would under-report spend. Choosing an explicit Delivery status overrides the card exclusion — if you asked to see archived ads, the cards describe archived ads.
  - **Rejected**: defaulting the Delivery filter to "Active only" so cards/table/Total always agree. Simpler and self-consistent, but ₹67 of real spend would vanish from the default view.
- **Delivery filter**: `DELIVERY_GROUPS` in `ad_performance.py` (active / paused / archived / deleted / removed / issues) with `filter_by_delivery()`. `paused` deliberately absorbs `CAMPAIGN_PAUSED` and `ADSET_PAUSED` — a dashboard reader doesn't care which level the pause came from. An unknown key returns rows unfiltered, so a stale bookmark shows the table rather than an empty one.
- **Split filtering**: the table filters client-side (instant, and the unfiltered set is needed both to build the dropdown's options and to count what the cards excluded); the CSV export passes `delivery_status` to the server so downloads match the screen. `DELIVERY_FILTERS` in `AdPerformanceTab.tsx` mirrors `DELIVERY_GROUPS` — a test asserts every frontend key exists server-side, since a mismatch would silently return an unfiltered CSV.
- The dropdown lists only statuses actually present in the data, so it can never offer a choice that empties the table.

## 2026-09-11 — KPI cards reverted to mirroring the Delivery filter

- **Reverses the card-scoping half of the entry above** (same day). The cards excluded ARCHIVED/DELETED while the Delivery filter sitting directly beneath them read "All statuses" — the control and the numbers contradicted each other on screen.
- **Decision**: cards, table rows and the Total all describe exactly one set: whatever the Delivery filter selects. No special-casing. Live-only numbers are now reached by choosing **Active** in the Delivery filter, which is explicit and self-describing.
- The filter still defaults to "All statuses", so the landing view counts archived ads. Deliberately not changed to default "Active" — that was not asked for, and it would silently hide rows the tenant has been looking at. Revisit if the ask comes.
- The "Live ads only — N archived ads left out" note under the cards is gone with the behaviour it explained.

## 2026-09-11 — `second_brain_close.py` stale-claim check fixed (21 false positives → 0)

- **Context**: The stale-claims check reported 21 findings on three consecutive session closes
  (2026-08-15, 2026-08-25, 2026-09-09) and **every one was a false positive** each time. It had
  started training readers to skip the section — the failure mode a health check can least afford.
- **Root cause**: the check flagged any backticked `path/like/this.py` not present on disk. But
  `.agents/` legitimately names absent files for five structural reasons, and the check could not
  tell any of them from a genuine stale claim.
- **Decision** — five exemptions added to `check_stale_claims()`:
  1. **A line documenting its own removal/rename.** `_REMOVAL_MARKERS` matches removed / renamed /
     deleted / dropped / parked / stripped / retired / replaced / superseded / reverted / gone /
     no longer / used to / formerly / gitignored, plus `→`, against the line the reference sits on.
     These sentences are the most valuable in the brain; flagging them inverted the test.
  2. **Self-declared append-only docs** are skipped wholesale (`"append-only"` in the first 600
     chars — `decisions/log.md` says so in its own header). A July entry naming a file deleted in
     August was true when written. Current-state docs (`context/*`) are still checked.
  3. **Leading-slash tokens** are URL routes (`/intake/sessions.csv`), not repo paths — repo paths
     in these notes are written without one. The old code `lstrip("/")`-ed them into false hits.
  4. **Elided paths** containing `...` (`frontend/.../performance-view.tsx`).
  5. **Never-committed artifacts** by extension (`.jks`, `.keystore`, `.p12`, `.apk`, `.aab`,
     `.env`) and **third-party module paths** whose first segment matches a dependency name read
     from `backend/requirements.txt` / `frontend/package.json` (caught `slowapi/middleware.py`).
- **Verified not over-suppressed**: dropped a throwaway `.agents/context/_tmp_probe.md` naming a
  fabricated `services/totally_made_up_module.py` — still reported. Probe deleted.
- The three duplicate backlog entries tracking this are marked FIXED rather than left live. The
  2026-08-15 entry had already proposed almost exactly this fix; it just never got built.
- **Still open, environment-level (not fixed, flagged again)**: `lefthook` is not on PATH so git
  hooks silently no-op, and `gitleaks` is not installed so the close-out credential scan falls back
  to four narrow patterns. Both are long-standing on this Windows workspace — see active-backlog.

**2026-09-16 — TeleCMI CHUB audited against the full docs; five conformance bugs fixed; calling still blocked upstream**
- **Decision**: Audited the whole CHUB integration against all 99 pages of `doc.telecmi.com/chub` (mirrored and read, not recalled), fixed every divergence, and stopped short of working around the remaining blocker because it is not ours. Commits `feea131b` (two-leg CDR + recording endpoint) and `d100cb20` (audio container detection). 1635 tests pass; each fix was mutation-tested by reintroducing the bug and confirming a test fails.
- **Rationale**: Outbound click2call initiation conformed field-for-field. Everything *after* a call ended did not, because the CDR handler was written against an assumed single-CDR model while CHUB documents — and really sends — two legs per call. That made final call status depend on CDR arrival order, took duration from the agent leg (which includes the customer's ring time), and metered `call_minute` once per leg, double-billing every call. The recording download pointed at `piopiy.telecmi.com/v1/play?...token=`, a different TeleCMI product's API, when the CHUB docs specify `rest.telecmi.com/v2/play?...secret=`; and because `/v2/play` returns HTTP 200 with a JSON error body on failure, `raise_for_status()` passed and the error blob was stored as the call's audio and handed to transcription, logged as "Recording saved". Also fixed: `record_url`/`recording_url` don't exist in the API (CHUB sends `filename`, leg B only), `bilsec` isn't a field, `status: 'user_missed'` isn't a status, six of nine live-event status strings weren't real, CDR lead-matching compared a bare number against `+91`-prefixed `leads.phone` so it never matched and silently created a duplicate lead per CDR, and recordings are `.wav` while the pipeline assumed mp3 in three places.
- **What is NOT fixed, and is not ours**: click2call is refused on both apps on the account — `Follow-me calls are not allowed for this app` on `33337312`, `You are not allowed to make call` on `33337317`. Credentials, user IDs, agent mobile, allowed caller IDs, countries, status and Allow PSTN were all verified correct, with wrong-value controls (407/404) proving the checks are real. Raised with TeleCMI. See subsystem-notes.md for the full evidence and the diagnostic commands.
- **Process note worth keeping**: three of my own hypotheses were disproven by the next test — app-level permission, then the per-user Follow Me toggle, then per-app balance. The per-app `balance` field read 0 for an app that was placing charged calls the same day; a `type:["request"]` Render log query returned empty because this service emits no request logs, which briefly produced a wrong "no CDR has ever arrived" conclusion. Controls (deliberately wrong secret/user) and reading the provider's own report API are what actually settled each question. Prefer those over inference from a single field or an empty query.


**2026-09-17 — Broadcast `fail_detail` filled for webhook failures; dead `send_status='delivery_failed'` update deleted, not enabled**
- **Decision**: Commit `ee717904`. The status webhook now writes Meta's `error_data.details` into `broadcast_recipients.fail_detail`, so the failed CSV explains webhook-reported failures (previously only send-time rejections had a detail). The update that set `send_status='delivery_failed'` was removed.
- **Rationale**: The live CHECK constraint (migration 166) rejects `'delivery_failed'`, so that update had failed on every delivery failure since it was written — and bundling `fail_detail` into it (the first draft) would have silently dropped the detail too; only the live `list constraints` query caught this, not the tests. Enabling the status via migration was rejected after tracing readers: ~8 sites (leads list, re-engagement, History tab, `template_performance`) equate `send_status='sent'` with "this lead was messaged", so relabelling would hide leads with healthy numbers whenever the *account* fails — 74 of the 110 live failures were 131042 payment issues. Failure reporting already derives from `messages.delivery_status` via `nearest_status`, so deleting changes no numbers. User chose delete (option 1) after the tradeoff was explained with a concrete lead example.
- **Process note**: `subsystem-notes.md` already recorded this update as dead code, and I didn't read that section until the close-out. It also carried a wrong reason (claimed recipient rows lack `meta_message_id`; live data showed all present) — corrected. Read the subsystem section before investigating, and treat its claims as leads to verify, not facts.

**2026-09-18 — Knowledge Auto-Sort: one upload place, split into Description (rules) and RAG (facts), client-approved**
- **Why**: live check on Astro Tamil — `business_description` was 2,005 chars of a pasted ChatGPT reply (no business identity at all), while the two RAG docs (60,615 chars) were ~95% behaviour rules and ~2,500 chars of real facts. Clients sort by file type ("it's a .docx → Documents"), not by function, and rule-docs in RAG poison replies (see subsystem-notes). Hand-writing a Description per client doesn't scale.
- **Decision**: uploads are sorted by the tenant's own model into RULE/FACT/MIXED/JUNK; rules are compiled into a proposed Description, facts kept verbatim; the client approves on a review screen (tick/untick hunks, pick one of conflicting rules, accept price updates). Storage stays split (Description always-on, RAG looked up) — only the input is unified. Version history for the Description and each file's facts. Deleted lines/facts come back if the same file is re-uploaded (user's call). "Does this replace an existing file?" picker + same-name prompt instead of name matching (clients rename "v9"→"v10"). Campaign-scoped files: rules left out (option A). Upload lock → Description-or-upload (see subsystem-notes).
- **Files**: migration `190_knowledge_auto_sort.sql`; `services/knowledge_sort.py`, `knowledge_versions.py`, `knowledge_sections.py`, `description_diff.py`; `routes/knowledge.py`, `routes/ai_tune.py`; `_llm_chat` gained `purpose`/`temperature`; frontend `dashboard/knowledge/` (3 modals + `descriptionDiff.ts` mirror + page wiring).
- **Verification**: 87 new/updated backend tests + 10 vitest; related backend test files all pass (two pre-existing files, `test_catalog.py`/`test_expert_handoff.py`, hang in the no-network sandbox on main too); `tsc` clean except pre-existing `lib/supabase/client.ts` (stale local `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.103.3 vs `^2.116.0`); `next lint` 0 errors. Modals and the Documents list rendered in Chrome via a temporary mocked route and screenshotted. **Not live-tested**: real LLM labelling/compile quality (sandbox has no network) — batch sizes (`_LABEL_BATCH_CHARS=12_000`, `_COMPILE_BATCH_CHARS=16_000`) are untuned guesses.
- **Security review (2026-09-18, general-purpose agent standing in for `security-reviewer`, which isn't installed)**: one MEDIUM found and fixed — a directly-inserted `knowledge_reviews` row (owner write policy + a `document_id` FK that doesn't check tenancy) could make `apply_review` schedule `_index_chunks` on another tenant's document, whose chunk delete had no tenant filter. Fixed three ways: review paths verify the document belongs to the tenant; `_index_chunks` delete filters `tenant_id`; the two new tables are SELECT-only under RLS (backend is the only writer). Deferred LOWs: `knowledge.view` can read Description history (app_settings was already member-readable); non-owner-line change/remove hunks are pre-ticked per the approved spec (a crafted upload could pre-tick edits — the owner still reviews); no `max_length` on `PUT /ai-tune/description` (existing contract); no rate limit on `/resort` or upload.
- **Migration 190 applied live 2026-09-18** via `apply_migration`; verified columns, SELECT-only policies, RLS on, and all 3 existing documents still legacy (`sorted_at` null). Code not yet deployed — the branch is local.


**2026-09-19 — Meta App Review outcome, and scheduler intervals cut below the Render restart floor**
- **Review result**: `whatsapp_business_messaging`, `whatsapp_business_management` and `public_profile` **renewed** — the core product is unaffected. **Rejected**: `ads_read` and the Marketing API standard access tier.
- **`ads_read` rejection was the screencast, not the use case.** Meta stated the use case *is* allowed but the video failed to show the end-to-end flow (Developer Policy 1.6): Meta login, the user granting the permission, then the feature working. Their item 5 is the relevant one — Aira's ads path uses a pasted **System User token**, so a recording shows no Meta login at all. Resubmission needs either a recording of the Facebook-Login-for-Business path (`_AD_ACCOUNT_GRANT_SCOPES` in `meta_cloud.py:191` already requests `ads_read`/`ads_management` through embedded signup) or an explicit server-to-server note in the submission, and preferably both. This one does **not** depend on call volume and can be resubmitted alone.
- **Marketing API tier rejection was factually correct.** Meta cited insufficient Ads API calls in the trailing 15 days. Confirmed against our own data: only 2 tenants ever configured ads credentials, newest row in `ad_insights_daily` dated 2026-08-25, and the last write (2026-09-17 18:03) landed seconds after a `meta_ads_account_id` update — i.e. a manual "Sync now", not the scheduler. No appeal wording changes a counter; genuine calls have to accrue first.
- **Root cause of the missing calls**: the ~5h Render restart cadence starves long interval jobs — see the new bullet at the top of [subsystem-notes Deployment (Render)](../context/subsystem-notes.md).
- **Changes made**: `ad-insights-sync` `hours=6` → `hours=1` (`0e35b2f1`); `token-health-check` and `number-quality-sync` `hours=24` → `hours=4` (`7aa8b710`). Kept as two commits — different systems, different blast radii, independently revertible. Backend suite: 1751 passed.
- **Deliberately not done**: not switched to a Render Cron Job (new service + deploy config for a marginal gain over an interval that now fits), and no `next_run_time` startup run (no other job in `main.py` uses that idiom).
- **Severity split worth remembering**: number quality has a live path regardless of the job — Meta pushes `phone_number_quality_update` over the webhook (`webhook.py:475`) and `POST /numbers/sync-from-meta` is a manual fallback — so the daily job is only a reconciler there. **Token health has no push equivalent**; Meta never announces an expired token, so while that job was starved nothing was proactively checking, and the first symptom would have been a failed send.


**2026-09-20 — Knowledge: the last apply block removed, Description moved onto the upload card**
- **Decision**: `apply_review()` no longer refuses an Apply that leaves the Description empty (the two lines raising `EmptyDescriptionError` are gone from `knowledge_sort.py`); the review screen warns instead, and the Documents tab grew an inline, owner-only Description box inside the "Add Knowledge Document" card.
- **Rationale**: uploads stopped being gated on the Description on 2026-09-18, but an FAQ-only first upload still dead-ended at a greyed-out Apply — the one case the split into "rules for the Description" vs "facts to look up" makes routine. Blocking it bought less than it cost: an empty Description is *degraded, not broken*. `_build_base_prompt()` just omits the `BUSINESS DESCRIPTION:` block, so the developer-owned master prompt still governs the reply — the assistant answers from RAG without knowing whose business it is. That is a quality problem the client should be told about, not a wall.
- **Why one field in two places, not two fields**: the inline box binds to the same `description` state and `saveDescription()` as the Description tab, so they cannot drift and no new endpoint exists. The guide, templates and version history stay on the Description tab; the inline box links to them.
- **Owner-only, and not for the obvious reason**: the whole `ai_tune` router is `require_owner`, so even reading the Description 403s for a manager and `loadDescription()` swallows it — `savedDescription` is `""` for every non-owner no matter what is stored. Showing them a "Not written yet" warning would be a lie. See [subsystem-notes.md](../context/subsystem-notes.md) → Knowledge base.
- **Kept deliberately**: `restore_version()` still refuses to restore an empty Description version (`EmptyDescriptionError` lives on for it) — a different button with different intent, out of scope. The stale-review 409 and the owner-only rule on Description changes are untouched; they are still hard blocks.
- **Copy that had to change with it**: the start-here hint said "Description (needed before a file goes live)", which the change makes false. It is now a rubric-only nudge, and the Description signal is the pill on the new box.
- **Verification**: backend 81 knowledge tests pass (`test_an_empty_result_is_refused` rewritten as `test_an_faq_only_first_upload_applies_and_leaves_the_description_empty`); frontend `next lint` clean on both changed files, vitest 81/81, and the new card was rendered and screenshotted at 1440px and 390px via a throwaway route outside `/dashboard` (auth has no bypass by design, so the real page cannot be screenshotted without credentials).


**2026-09-20 — Knowledge: Documents (RAG) leads, and the split is explained on the page**
- **Decision**: the tab order and default landing tab flipped to `Documents (RAG)` → `Description`, and a small always-visible `SplitGuide` card now sits under the guide links on **both** tabs, stating what each half is for and what one upload does.
- **Rationale**: user feedback after using the rebuilt page — "the UI is quite confusing… two upload options". Two separate faults. (1) Ordering: Description led because it used to be the prerequisite for uploading; that gate is gone, so leading with it made the page open on the thing clients rarely came for. (2) Nothing on screen said how the two halves differ, so the inline Description box added earlier the same day read as a *second upload* inside the card labelled "Add Knowledge Document".
- **REVERTED the same day (2026-09-20): the `SplitGuide` card was removed at the user's request.** Only the tab-order half of this entry shipped. The two collapsed guide links on each tab are unchanged and are again the only on-page explanation of the split. If this comes up a third time, do not re-add a card here without asking what specifically was wrong with it — it was removed without a stated reason, so "they want it explained on the page" is not a safe assumption. The original rationale, kept for context:
- **The guide was deliberately not collapsible.** Both tabs already have two collapsed guides behind links; the split is the one thing clients get wrong, and a third collapsed guide would have been no more visible than the two nobody opens. Three short lines: Description = read before *every* reply; Documents = read *only when a lead asks*; one upload feeds both, nothing goes live until you review it.
- **Rejected**: three fuller redesigns were offered (flip only; flip + tabs renamed to plain language e.g. "What Aira looks up"; one page with two numbered collapsible sections and no tabs). The user rejected all three and asked for exactly these two changes. The renaming idea is still live if the jargon ("RAG", "Description") proves to be the real barrier — it was not tried.
- **Kept**: the inline Description box on the upload card stays. It was not the ordering problem, and it is what gives a phone user any route to the Description at all (see below).
- **Mobile consequence worth knowing**: the header pills are `hidden … md:flex`, so a phone shows no tabs. Before this change a phone landed on Description and could not reach Documents; now it lands on Documents and reaches the Description only through the `goToDescription()` buttons inside that tab. That is a net improvement, but it means those buttons are load-bearing on mobile, not decorative.
- **Four places encode the default tab**, not the three the old note claimed: the pill array order, the `isActive` derivation, `getPageMeta()`'s `tabLabel` default, and `goToDescription()` in `page.tsx` (which had to change from deleting the param to setting it). See [subsystem-notes.md](../context/subsystem-notes.md) → Frontend.
- **Verification**: `next lint` clean, vitest 81/81, tsc unchanged at the one pre-existing `lib/supabase/client.ts` error from `721f58e4`. Both tabs rendered and screenshotted at 1440px and 390px.


**2026-09-20 — Knowledge UI: one upload route, description reduced to a status row, modal backdrop fixed**
- **Context**: a run of small user-driven corrections after the auto-sort rework, all from the same complaint — the page looked like it offered several ways to put content in. Worth recording because two of them reverse decisions made earlier the same day.
- **One upload route.** The empty-state card's `Upload First Document` button is gone; the drop zone is the only upload affordance. The drop zone was kept over the button because it also carries drag-and-drop, the `Replaces:` selector, the campaign-scope selector and the formats list — the button was only ever a second door to the same file picker. Empty-state copy now says "using the box above".
- **Filter/search toolbar hidden when `documents.length === 0`.** You could previously filter an empty knowledge base and end up with an `Active Filters: Type: Word ✕ Reset all` chip sitting above "No documents in your knowledge base yet". **Gated on `documents`, not `filteredDocs`** — gating on the filtered list would make the toolbar delete itself the moment a filter matched nothing, removing the only control that could undo it. Known residue: filter state is not reset when the list empties, so deleting every document and uploading a new one can return to a stale active filter; the "No documents match your filters" message names the cause, so it was left.
- **The inline Description editor became a status row** (word count + button to the Description tab). It had been an editable copy of the same textarea, added hours earlier to solve the FAQ-only dead-end. Two editors for one field is what made the card read as two kinds of input. There is now exactly one editor for the description, on its own tab.
- **Modal backdrops now cover the whole viewport.** Two independent bugs, both measured in Chrome rather than guessed: a `space-y-6` page root injected `margin-top: 24px` into every `fixed inset-0` overlay (24px undimmed strip under the app header), and `backdrop-blur-xs` does not exist in Tailwind 3.4 so there had never been any blur. See [subsystem-notes.md](../context/subsystem-notes.md) → Frontend for both, including how to tell this apart from a z-index bug.
- **Process note worth keeping**: three fuller redesigns of this page were offered as a clickable artifact canvas (flip order / rename tabs to plain language / one page with numbered sections). The user rejected all three and steered with single-sentence instructions instead. A `SplitGuide` explainer card built on request was removed by the same user two turns later. For a page this settled, small targeted edits landed and redesign menus did not.


**2026-09-22 — Calling setup moved from the Team page to Roles; Team is now read-only**
- **Decision**: telecaller cards in Team → Live Agent Status lost their inline phone/User-ID pencils and their delete button; the Admin card lost its pencils too (asked for in a follow-up). Roles → Users is now the single place any calling identity is edited. Deleting a telecaller stays where it already was — Roles → Users, which cascades to the `callers` row.
- **The owner could not reuse the RBAC path.** `PATCH /api/v1/rbac/users/{id}` hard-rejects the owner (`rbac.py` — "Owner role cannot be edited here"), and `_ensure_caller_profile` would *deactivate* the owner's `callers` row anyway, because `owner` is not a telecaller role. So the Master row's new **Admin Calling Setup** mode writes through `PATCH /api/v1/callers/{id}` instead, which is already owner-only (`get_owner_tenant_id`) and tenant-scoped. **No backend change was needed or made.**
- **Consequences worth knowing**: the pencil on the Master row is gated on the *viewer* being `role === "owner"`, not on `canWrite` — a `roles.manage` holder would get a 403 from the callers endpoint. The owner's `callers` row is auto-seeded at tenant creation (`onboarding.py`, `operator.py`), so gating the button on `caller_profile` existing is safe; a legacy tenant without one silently shows no button.
- **Also in this pass**: each Users row now shows the configured phone in full and the agent id masked (`5141_••••••12`) — 6 characters hidden via a `MASKED_CHARS` constant, keeping the *extension* prefix visible because that is the part that differs per person and the part you need to read when diagnosing a `404 invalid user_id`. The owner's second badge was renamed "Boss account" → "Admin". A "Calling setup incomplete" chip appears on the Master row when phone (or, under `telecmi`, the agent id) is missing.
- **Verification**: `next lint` clean and `tsc` unchanged at the one pre-existing `lib/supabase/client.ts` error. **Not rendered** — no dashboard login available in the session, so all of this is compiler-verified only.

**2026-09-23 — Master Prompt is Platform-Wide (no per-tenant copy)**
- **Decision**: Every tenant now reads the single `platform_defaults.default_master_prompt` row at reply time via `get_master_prompt()`. Removed per-tenant seeding at client creation, the per-client edit on the operator Configuration tab (now read-only, with a link to Operator → Master Prompt), and the prompt writes in the onboarding starter/interview flow — those now set only `business_description`. `ai_prompts` is no longer read anywhere; `_get_prompt()` is gone. Operator nav item renamed "Default Prompt" → "Master Prompt".
- **Rationale**: Operator decision — behaviour should be uniform across clients; only the business description varies. The old copy-on-create model meant editing the template changed nothing for live clients, and 3 of 5 tenants had silently diverged (Astro Tamil 13.5k chars, Bloom Matrix 4.7k, Astro Tamil - Co 906 — vs the 14,376-char platform text). **This changed live reply behaviour for those three on deploy.**
- **Follow-up, same day**: on the operator's instruction ("remove all other prompts, just keep the current master prompt and don't store anything") the `ai_prompts` table was dropped entirely — migration `198_drop_ai_prompts.sql`, applied live — taking all 9 rows with it. **No backup was taken; Astro Tamil's 13,518-char prompt is unrecoverable.** The operator was shown the full row inventory and chose the drop over saving a copy to a file. Also removed: `active_prompt` from `GET /api/v1/system/status`, `ai_prompts` from `delete_client`'s sweep list, and the sim harness's `--master-from-tenant` flag (now `--master-from-file`, since there are no per-tenant rows to read).


**2026-09-23 — `PAID_INTAKE_DIAGNOSIS.md` archived: a 2026-09-09 diagnosis report, mostly fixed, migrated into `.agents/` by a second-brain audit**
- **Context**: a root-level standalone diagnosis report (D1–D14, `pytest` transcripts against a mocked DB) covering two symptoms — the paid-intake offer canceling on any non-exact-affirmative reply, and quick-reply button taps failing to match when `button_label` differs from the package `name`. Its own status line said D1–D10, D12, D13 were fixed and verified as of 2026-09-09; only D6 (refund/expiry webhook events), D11 (a no-op dead-arg cleanup, correctly deferred — fixing it would break test assertions in 4 unrelated files for no functional gain) were left open. Never referenced from any router or `.agents/` file, so it wasn't part of the queryable brain.
- **Verified during the audit, not just trusted**: `intake.py:516,547` (`node.get("button_label") or node["name"]`) and `webhook.py:680`'s comment confirm D2's fix (match on the button's `id`/`key` first, which survives label truncation) is live. `intake.py:622/627/1520` all include `awaiting_addon_choice` in `_ACTIVE_STATUSES`/`_IN_PROGRESS_STATUSES`/the third status list, confirming D5. Not exhaustively re-verified for every one of D1/D3/D4/D7/D8/D9/D10/D12/D13 — trusting the report's own "fixed and verified" line for those, since two of the two spot-checked were both correct.
- **What was carried forward, not just deleted**: D6's still-open items (no `expire_by` on link creation, only `payment_link.paid` handled on the webhook, empty `callback_url`, refund events unhandled) are now in [active-backlog.md](../projects/active-backlog.md) next to the pre-existing "No Razorpay refund path exists" item, which they extend rather than duplicate. D11 was left out of the backlog on purpose — the report itself sized it as no functional bug, not worth the test churn.
- **Why archived rather than left in place**: the report is a point-in-time diagnosis, not living documentation like the consultation bridge docs. Both `ConsultationBridgeGuide.md` and `ConsultationAPIEndpoints.md` were later deleted at the user's direction (2026-09-23); the wire contract now lives in `subsystem-notes.md`'s AstroTamil bridge section. Keeping a "diagnosis" doc around after its findings are fixed or migrated just invites someone to re-read it as current state.

**2026-09-23 — Sender number name is Meta-owned: dashboard rename removed, sync made authoritative**
- **Decision**: the inline pencil on Numbers → Active Pool is gone, and both Meta sync paths now overwrite `phone_numbers.display_name` with Meta's `verified_name` (previously only inserts did). The name shown on a number card is now exactly what customers see in WhatsApp, with a tooltip saying it's set in WhatsApp Manager and pulled in by Sync.
- **Why**: the pencil wrote a purely local label — nothing ever reached Meta — so the dashboard could show "Sales line" while every customer saw "Astro". Asked about it, the user chose removal over building a real rename.
- **Why not build the real rename**: Meta's `new_display_name` API is a submit-for-review flow (PENDING_REVIEW → approval webhook → mandatory re-register within 14 days, 10 changes per 30 days), and the re-register step needs a 2-step PIN we generate randomly and discard, with no Meta API to reset it. Full mechanics and the PIN blocker are in [subsystem-notes.md](../context/subsystem-notes.md) → "Display name on a number is Meta's, not ours".
- **Removed on all three layers, not just the UI**: the pencil, `display_name` on the frontend `numbersApi.update` type, and `display_name` on the backend `UpdatePhoneNumber` model. A `PATCH` carrying only a name now 400s. Keeping the API field "for manually-created numbers" was my first instinct and was the wrong call — there is no manual-add UI either, so it was dead surface that sync would overwrite regardless (see memory: total removal, not leftovers). `POST /numbers` keeps its `display_name` because a row needs a label before its first sync.
- **Verification**: 2 new tests in `test_numbers_sync_from_meta.py` (stale label overwritten; missing `verified_name` leaves the name alone rather than blanking it). Full backend suite 1829 passed. `next lint` clean on the file, `tsc` unchanged at the one pre-existing `lib/supabase/client.ts` error. **Not rendered** — no dashboard login in session, so the card with the pencil gone is compiler-verified only.

**2026-09-24 — Scoring is Hot/Warm/Cold (no number); one generic master prompt; fixed-section profiles; measured with three eval harnesses**
- **Decision (scoring):** the arc score + keyword/engagement bonuses are gone. `scoring_engine._classify_segment()` asks the LLM for hot/warm/cold against fixed definitions (Hot = a buying step). The tenant rubric is now 3 lines of business examples and cannot redefine them. `leads.score` is still written (A=9,B=6,C=2,D=0) for the DB CHECK/exports; UI shows a segment badge; lists sort segment then `last_inbound_at`. Every drop needs two confirming messages; a clear refusal opts the lead out (`opted_out`) — the AI's "you won't be contacted again" was untrue for 7/11 Astro Tamil Not-Interested leads. Old 1–10 rubrics are converted in code (`convert_5band_rubric`) — the generic default put ~everything in Cold.
- **Decision (master prompt):** operator-approved 2026-09-24. The published master prompt is domain-neutral; Astro Tamil's astrology/birth-detail rules moved into the three Astro Tamil tenants' own profiles FIRST, because removing them from the master would otherwise have silently dropped them for those tenants. Tanglish style guide moved to `ai_reply._TANGLISH_STYLE_GUIDE`, attached to the language setting. Reply language is decided only by the operator-console setting (Tamil Nadu audience, deliberate — don't generalise mirroring to other languages).
- **Decision (profile):** the description is edited as 6 fixed headed sections (`business_profile.py`, 700-word hard cap), still stored as the same text. Handover is a separate per-client `handover_line` setting; the 6 hardcoded "a team member will contact you" phrases now point at one HANDOVER RULE. Astro Tamil's "no office, everything through the app" and "each consultation paid separately" are facts the operator supplied.
- **Why measured:** three harnesses under `backend/evals/` (scoring 74%→95% accuracy; replies 86%→99%; upload pipeline: contact lines leaking into the profile 5/5→0). Do not change the classifier prompt, master prompt or sorter without rerunning the relevant one. Real-chat fixtures are gitignored (customer data).
- **Traps found by measuring:** the rubric generator read only the first 1,500 chars of the description (44% of Astro Tamil's profile) — now 12,000; the model invented a phone number for a suggested handover (`verified_handover()` now forces the file's own sentence); a security-review finding on the teammate's middleware fast path (JWT trusted by expiry only) fixed with `getClaims()`; `propose_conversion` sometimes marks business rules as "already in platform rules" — a human must read its "removed" list.
- **Migration:** 201 (`knowledge_reviews.suggested_handover`), applied live before the code shipped (additive, defaulted).
- **Not done / open:** Astrotamil Pooja has no handover line and lacks its Homam date/place/booking details; rubric auto-update is Off for all three tenants; Jina key is shared by the three Astro Tamil tenants (same paying client) — separate clients each need their own.


**2026-09-24 — Aira Business Kit (Stage 1): one 8-heading shape for every client; templates + AI prompt + readiness line**
- **Decision:** self-serve clients get one document shape (8 headings) through three doors: copy our prompt into their own AI, download an industry Word template (12 industries, all invented example businesses — no client's material is reused), or upload anything. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-24-aira-business-kit-design.md`.
- **Placement (user: "make it simple"):** two buttons under the drop zone while the client has no documents, one quiet link after; the owner-only Description status row became a readiness line (`GET /knowledge/readiness`, knowledge.view, no AI call). The RAG explainer card and both guide/template panels were deleted.
- **Kit files are routed by heading, not by AI label** (`knowledge_kit.split_kit` + `knowledge_sort.label_document`) — measured, not assumed: `evals/knowledge_sort/run_kit_eval.py` sorted 3/12 industry files cleanly before (short "About" packed with the title, labelled FACT, 9/12 Descriptions had no ABOUT US) and 12/12 after, on two runs.
- **Safety:** `strip_example` cuts the template's example below its marker and `scrub_placeholders` drops `[OWNER TO CHECK]` / unfilled `[WRITE ...]` lines before sorting (shown to the client as "You left this blank"). The template's "how to use" box is a Word table, which `extract_text_from_file` never reads.
- **Not done:** Stage 2 in-app question form (build only if clients without an AI habit get stuck); non-English Kit.

## 2026-09-25 - Deals & Products mini-CRM (one deal model for every source)
- **Why:** production had silently skipped migrations 191–197 (applied 190, then 198+), so the Pipeline tab hung on skeletons, every catalog item create/edit 500'd (price/stock columns missing) and IndiaMART/JustDial leads were rejected by the `leads.source` CHECK. The never-applied `catalog_quotes` design also upserted one row per lead, overwriting a repeat buyer's earlier sale.
- **Decision (user-approved):** one `deals` table for every source (whatsapp AI, intake form, call, walk-in, manual, indiamart, justdial) with `deal_items` (price/GST snapshot) and `stock_movements` (every stock change logged via the `apply_stock_movement` RPC). Stages quoted → awaiting_payment → won | lost; **Won is final** (no Delivered/Resolved column). Stock deducts on won only; won→lost returns it. Automation choice B: the AI's `send_quote` creates an awaiting_payment deal and the Razorpay link goes out in the same reply, no staff step. Intake stays the question-asking engine and mirrors onto its deal (`deals.sync_intake_session`, called from `intake._sync_deal` using UPDATE-returned rows). GST optional; each tenant chooses prices-include-GST (default yes) under Settings → Business details (`app_settings.business_details`, NOT `services/business_profile.py`, which is the AI prompt's description sections).
- **Renames:** sidebar Intake → Deals (`/dashboard/deals`, `/dashboard/intake` redirects to its Form answers tab), Catalog → Products (route unchanged).
- **Migrations applied live 2026-09-25:** 192, 193, 194 (rewritten: price only), 195 (rewritten: DROP + CREATE `match_catalog_items` — CREATE OR REPLACE can't change return columns — with `search_path` restored), 204_deals. 196/197 deleted from the repo, never applied anywhere.
- **Marketplace:** IndiaMART reads the `RESPONSE` envelope (the old code read top-level keys and silently dropped every real push). New enquiry → lead + note with what the buyer typed + tenant's welcome template (`marketplace_welcome_template` = message_templates id) + immediate assignment (segment gate skipped, telecalling on/off respected). Repeat enquiry → note only.

## 2026-09-26 — TeleCMI call scoring v4 — CallIQ Steps 1-2
- **Q1:** Build in phases; Phase 1 = Step 1 (sort calls) + Step 2 (mark a real conversation out of 100) only.
- **Q2:** Talk share is computed by words from the transcript; interruptions come from the two audio tracks (real overlap), with "telecaller keeps talking ≥~1s" standing in for the spec's "≥3 words".
- **Q3:** The CRM check (check 10 + the early-exit check) uses the existing wrap-up (outcome, manual status, do-not-call, callback date, notes) — "interested" is accepted for both Hot and Warm; manual outcome marking itself is unchanged.
- **Q4:** The nine AI checks are marked as soon as the recording is processed; check 10 is marked when the wrap-up is saved. The score shows provisional until then; no wrap-up within 2 hours → check 10 = Missing and the score goes final.
- **Q5:** The admin "which criteria to score" setting and the audio Tone check are removed completely.
- **Q6/Q8:** Every transcript line carries its time in the call; each audio track is transcribed separately (not one merged pass) because the AI swapped telecaller/customer on the 2026-09-25 test call when transcribed together.
- **Q7:** All warnings go to the admin only (no "manager" role) via a Needs attention list + sidebar count + one morning summary; instant alerts only for rudeness and wrong product information, capped at 1 per telecaller per hour. Telecallers see their own warnings on the call card only.
- **Q9:** Proof quotes (checks, signs, warnings) are sent to the browser clipped to 240 chars for both telecaller and admin; the full transcript stays masked (first/last line only), as before.
- **Migrations:** 205 (applied live 2026-09-26 — `call_logs` columns for the v4 flow + the new `call_alerts` table) and 206 (applied live 2026-09-26 — widened `call_logs_score_status_check` to old ∪ new score_status values so v4 writes wouldn't 500 pre-deploy). **207 PENDING, post-deploy only**: drops `flag_status`/`flag_reason`/`flagged_at`/`flag_resolved_by`/`flag_resolved_at`/`score_breakdown`; maps old `score_status` values (`short_call`→`very_short`, `no_answer`→`not_connected`, `pending`/`awaiting_outcome`/`no_recording`→`processing`); narrows the check to the new set only.
- **Real-AI check (2026-09-26):** test call `ed296fa2` re-scored with the real AI from a local run against the live DB. First run exposed three problems (casual chat sorted as a real conversation, marks differing between runs, the string "null" treated as a quote); fixed by giving sorting the business knowledge base, a stricter next-step sign, majority-of-3 AI votes for sorting, marking and the wrap-up check (user chose option A), and treating "null" as no quote. Two re-runs after the fix: both Early exit, 0 signs. Stale alerts from the first run deleted.

## 2026-09-26 — Aira sells by itself: AI-native selling engine replaces the intake state machine (default for every tenant)
- **Why:** the fixed `route_intake` script intercepted turns before the AI. Live failures: an empty "confirm your details" after a package tap (tenant with no required fields), and a customer who said they paid getting menus and "contact app support" for 5 minutes. Owner decision: no per-tenant switch, the AI handles packages, details, links, products and handoff for everyone.
- **What:** `services/deal_engine.py` (prompt + schemas + price check), `deal_actions.py` (guarded executors for package AND catalog tools), `deal_turn.py` (one guarded loop for every reply); webhook no longer calls `route_intake`; settings pages merged. Full notes: subsystem-notes.md "AI-native selling engine".
- **Kept:** intake_sessions, Deals board sync, Razorpay confirm, Astro bridge, staleness sweep, 24h window handling (owner: deliberately unchanged).
- **Measured:** conversation evals in `backend/evals/conversations/` (see docs/plans/ai-native-conversation.md for numbers).

## 2026-09-26 — Choices always tappable, in-chat handover, Services page as source of truth, dashboard regrouped
- **Why:** the owner asked that any choice Aira offers always arrives as quick-reply buttons, that handovers keep the lead in chat, and that the Description/knowledge never contradict packages (Astro Tamil: Description said "from ₹29, everything in the app, never ask DOB" while selling ₹49/₹99 in chat and collecting DOB).
- **What:** `services/choices.py` + `offer_choices` tool + code backstops; `hand_to_human` for every tenant, team-claim and asked-again guards; `services/consistency.py` + `routes/consistency.py` (report in app_settings `consistency_report`); dashboard: Services moved to the main sidebar (`/dashboard/services`), settings grouped (Account / Channels / Aira / Calling / Alerts), Profile & Business merged (`/dashboard/settings/account`), TeleCMI credentials inside Connect Channels, Business hours on the Knowledge page, old routes redirect.
- **Operator console secret list** unified in `config_dynamic.SECRET_SETTING_KEYS` (Razorpay secrets had been saved unmasked).

## 2026-09-27 — One place for each fact: app link and hours in the Description, Quick Replies removed, Business Details final
- **Why:** owner: separate fields for facts the Description already holds only make room for contradictions. Live proof: Astro Tamil's saved quick reply offered "Basic 100 / premium 200" against real ₹49/₹99 packages, and saved replies bypassed the price check.
- **What:** app link = first link in the Description (`ai_reply.business_app_link`, also used by the "answer ready" nudge); business hours live in the Description and the escalation block gets the current IST time instead (`services/business_hours.py` and its routes deleted); Quick Replies feature deleted (route, service, page, tool; `quick_reply_blocks` table kept, Astro's block set inactive); Business Details page is final and Aira shares a GST number, address, email or phone only when asked (`deal_engine.volunteered_details` refusal in `deal_turn`).
- **Dashboard:** Products icon is a shopping bag; Services page redesigned flat (no card, no duplicate title, sticky save bar).

## 2026-09-27 — Call wrap-up v2: one two-tap wrap-up for SIM and cloud
- **Why:** SIM and cloud had different outcome lists, stars/tags nobody used, and "Log the sale" was a separate step; the lead page had a second "Call Outcome" card doing the same job.
- **Decisions (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-call-wrapup-v2-design.md`, D1–D10):** two taps (connected? → what happened?), notes required for the three interested/converted results, Converted creates the Won deal, IST retry suggestions with the 3-failures rule, the lead-page card became "Send details on WhatsApp", cloud Hot/Warm/Cold is corrected by the AI's majority reading (admin sees it).
- **Interpretations made while planning:** "cancel others" = `apply_wrapup` directly cancels the lead's other pending phone/callback reminders itself (tenant-scoped, excluding the linked job), on every wrap-up — NOT the WhatsApp follow-up re-plan (`sync_follow_up_jobs` only replans WhatsApp cadence rows, and is a no-op while `FOLLOW_UP_CADENCES` is empty; corrected under Ruling W9, final review, after the original wording here wrongly assumed it cancelled every pending reminder). wrong_number and do_not_call still cancel every pending follow-up via `growth.cancel_pending_follow_ups` (now tenant-scoped) — the number isn't the customer / they asked for no contact. Dial-time and auto-created leads start as `trying`. Connect rate counts only wrapped-up calls.
- **Migrations:** 208 (pre-deploy: new columns, checks widened to old ∪ new) and 209 (post-deploy: old rows mapped — interested→interested_needs_time, callback→call_later, no_answer→NULL, SIM result statuses moved to outcome, in_progress→trying — checks narrowed, `wrapup_callback_at` and `quality_rating` dropped). 207 (scoring cleanup) is separate and still pending.
- **Deploy runbook (Ruling W11):**
  1. Confirm migration 208 is live (it already widened the `outcome`/`manual_status`/`leads.call_status` checks and added the new columns — verify before deploying code that writes the new values).
  2. Deploy backend (Render) and frontend (Vercel) together, same release — the wrap-up payload shape changed, so an old frontend tab talking to the new backend (or vice versa) sends/expects fields the other side doesn't know. A stale tab's save now gets HTTP 422 and shows "The app was updated — please refresh the page and save again." instead of silently corrupting a wrap-up.
  3. Telecallers hard-refresh any open dialer tabs right after the deploy (don't rely on them noticing the 422 toast on their own — tell them).
  4. Apply migration 209 immediately after the deploy is confirmed healthy, in the same session — it maps the old rows (`interested`→`interested_needs_time`, `callback`→`call_later`, `no_answer`→NULL, SIM result statuses, `in_progress`→`trying`) and narrows the checks; it is not safely re-runnable (guarded by Ruling W8) and old rows sitting un-mapped confuse the recycler and analytics in the meantime.
  5. Submit/approve the Meta templates `call_details_share` and `call_details_next_step` (WhatsApp template review can take hours to days) — without them, "Send details on WhatsApp" has no template to fall back on once a lead's 24h window is closed.

## 2026-09-28 — Agent setup: gstack sprint, unattended R&D team, token cleanup
- **Process:** gstack 1.91.2.0 (pinned in CLAUDE.md, `gstack-` prefix) is the one sprint; superpowers is off. `/gstack-ship`, `land-and-deploy`, `setup-deploy` were hidden from agents (`skillOverrides` in `.claude/settings.json`; only a person ran them) — SUPERSEDED 2026-09-29: the founder switched all six ship/deploy skill names to `on`, see the entry at the end of this file. Roles Aira never uses are off in the user's global settings (iOS, gbrain, pair-agent, benchmark-models, design-html, landing-report).
- **R&D team (scripts/rnd/, .agents/rnd/):** a nightly job at 01:30 (launchd until 2026-10-25, now a Claude desktop scheduled task — see the 2026-10-25 R&D entry) runs the night scouts (errors, silent replies/leaked internals, live UI, code health); Sundays also the strategy run on `.agents/rnd/charter.md`. Items the founder ticks (`- [x] Approve` in `rnd/reports/*-night*.md`, or the dashboard) are built next run on their own `rnd/*` branch; the runner commits, nothing is merged or pushed. `make rnd-dashboard` is the local UI (approve, merge/drop, push, run now). Reports are local only (`/rnd/` is gitignored: they hold aggregated customer patterns).
- **Two locks, both needed:** `scripts/rnd/fence.py` (a PreToolUse rule list, only when `AIRA_AUTONOMOUS=1`) can be bypassed by a script the agent writes, so `fence-settings.json` also turns on Claude Code's OS sandbox (network only to bloommatrix.in with `strictAllowlist`, no reads of .env/~/.claude settings, RENDER/SUPABASE/GH keys removed). Tests, typecheck and the live-UI browser checks run in `run.sh`, not in the agent's sandbox. Found by a security review of the first R&D commit; checked live against Render API, GitHub and a disguised .env read.
- **Never spend a client's money:** evals need `--test-key-tenant`; `make verify` skips them unless `AIRA_EVAL_KEY_TENANT` is a TEST tenant with its own key.
- **Token cost, measured (an empty session in Aira = ~59k tokens):** plugins, the skill listing and hooks cost ~0 to trim (the listing is budget-capped; do not go plugin-hunting again). Real levers: always-on rules (`~/.claude/rules/common` + `web` = ~9k), CLAUDE.md files (~2.6k), claude.ai connectors (~2.3k). Applied: 8 rule files excluded via `claudeMdExcludes` in `.claude/settings.json` (three of them told agents to auto-spawn planner/tdd/code-reviewer subagents, clashing with gstack) → ~-4.8k; connectors off only for night runs (`fence-settings.json`; NOT in shared settings, teammates may rely on the claude.ai Supabase connector) → another ~-2.1k; global `autoCompactWindow` 250000 so long sessions compact earlier. Measure with `claude -p "ok" --output-format json --settings '<json>'` and add `usage` token fields.
- **`.claude/settings.local.json` is tracked in git** (shared with keerthi) despite the name: put nothing personal in it.

- **Safe local backend mode (2026-09-29, `b4dfb349`)**: `make dev-backend` → `SCHEDULER_ENABLED=false` (APScheduler starts paused) + `OUTBOUND_MODE=dry_run` (`services/outbound_guard.py` patches httpx send; external writes logged + faked 200; `OUTBOUND_ALLOW_TO` lets listed recipients through). Chosen over a separate dev DB (no Docker/Supabase CLI; one live DB) and over relying on a test account alone (a test account does not stop the scheduler from messaging every tenant's leads). Production defaults unchanged, no Render env change. A test (`test_app_uses_no_http_client_the_guard_cannot_see`) fails if app code adds a non-httpx HTTP client the guard can't see.

- **Sentry removed (2026-09-29)**: owner confirmed it is unused. Backend init + `sentry-sdk` dep, frontend `@sentry/nextjs` + config files + `withSentryConfig` wrapper deleted. Earlier log entries mentioning Sentry are historical. Sentry no longer receives Platform Data (the Data Handling tab disclosure entry above is outdated on that point). Leftover `SENTRY_DSN` lines in render.yaml / backend/.env.example removed in `0464e1e9`; the value in Render's dashboard is harmless (Settings `extra=ignore`) and is deleted by hand — the Render MCP can only merge or replace ALL env vars, never delete one.

- **`/brief` added; ship/deploy skills switched ON (2026-09-29)**: `/brief <rough idea>` (`.claude/commands/brief.md`, Codex mirror `.agents/skills/source-command-brief/`, glossary `.agents/context/prem-glossary.md` = the founder's own words) turns a messy ask into a short brief (Goal, Done when, Must respect, Not doing, max 3 open questions), stops for "go", then saves it to `.agents/briefs/` and routes into the gstack sprint. `brief` is `user-invocable-only` in `skillOverrides` (~0 tokens per session; `/brief quick` gives a 5-line version). Proof run on 5 of the founder's real past messages (bug, feature, UI, rambling, one-liner): all 5 graded PASS; file/line claims in them spot-checked against the repo. Lookup budget was overrun by every test agent, so Step 3 now states a hard stop. Same day the founder set `ship`, `gstack-ship`, `land-and-deploy`, `gstack-land-and-deploy`, `setup-deploy`, `gstack-setup-deploy` to `on` after being told the trade-off: agents can now start a push or deploy (a push to `main` auto-deploys the backend on Render), so the only guard left is the rule in CLAUDE.md: push or deploy only when the user says so.

## 2026-09-30 — Aira Brain built: 8-section Description, conflicts in the hub only, handover data step, answers-only Test Aira (`95634b21`, `b8c9facb`, `e459d39d`; spec `docs/designs/aira-brain-blueprint.md`)
- **Description is 8 sections, all owner-only.** Added `hours_contact` (60 words) and `handover` (50 words). The handover line now lives in section 8 and is read through `business_profile.get_handover_line`; the legacy `handover_line` setting is only a fallback when the 8th heading is absent. Generic settings PATCH rejects `handover_line` and non-owner `business_description` writes; owner writes are versioned (Description history can undo them).

## 2026-09-30 — Settings accordion defaultOpenId & Profile & Business page layout migration
- **Why:** The Profile & Business settings page (`/dashboard/settings/account`) was previously rendered without `SettingsAccordion`, so all 3 sections (`Your profile`, `Sign-in & security`, and `Business details`) were expanded simultaneously and the app header lacked the `3 SECTIONS` accordion controls present on the Notifications settings page.
- **What:**

  - Wrapped `Profile & Business` in `<SettingsAccordion defaultOpenId="profile">`. "Your profile" is open by default, with "Sign-in & security" and "Business details" collapsed by default.
  - Removed redundant `<h1>` header block from `account/page.tsx` so the page begins directly with the cards, matching the layout of the Notifications page and connecting to `AppHeader`'s `3 SECTIONS` `1 open` | `Expand all` / `Collapse all` controls.
